2026-11-03

ARIDA - Active railway infrastructure dataset annotation using neural network predictions

ARIDA - Active railway infrastructure dataset annotation using neural network predictions

Maximilian Dornik、Christian Sieberichs、Simon Geerkens、Alexander Braun

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modern railway perception requires large comprehensive image datasets, but creating them is slowed by manual labeling, especially for track polylines and other complex infrastructure geometry. This paper presents the Active Railway Infrastructure Dataset Annotation framework, a railway-specific semi-automated pipeline that converts instance predictions from a mask scoring instance segmentation network into bounding-boxes, polygons, polylines, and paired polylines. To keep automatic track geometry annotation reliable without ground truth, it applies confidence thresholds and an unique contour-agreement quality score as an acceptance gate. An active learning loop ranks remaining images by uncertainty and adapts the number of manual queries per cycle. This is achieved through a novel quantity control mechanism, in which easy images are auto-accepted and human effort targets hard cases. Experiments on disjoint, diverse imagery and time-sequential, redundant imagery quantify effort reduction and accuracy trade-offs, achieving about two thirds reduction in the disjoint regime and up to 93% in the sequential regime. The study also provides guidance for initial label budgets and stopping to avoid over-annotation.

生成模型/扩散视频理解
2026-11-03

Spatio-Temporal Multi-Order Interaction for Video Recognition

Spatio-Temporal Multi-Order Interaction for Video Recognition

Kevin D. Nguetche、Lilian Hollard、Eric Granger、Marco Pedersoli

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Many temporal approaches for video recognition rely on explicit cross-channel aggregation projections, which have quadratic complexity in the number of channels, introducing significant computational overhead. In addition, the spatio-temporal representation used for video recognition is biased either toward local order interactions (for CNNs) or toward global order interactions (for transformers). Starting from the multi-order spatial representation of MogaNet, our method introduces two complementary contributions to tackle these problems. First, for the temporal modelling, we introduce parallel multi-order processing with depthwise-only convolutions and implicit gating-based aggregation, which is linear in the number of channels, inducing a considerable computational reduction. Second, we propose a dual interaction between spatial and temporal dimensions, which allows a better spatio-temporal representation without increasing the computational cost. By combining these two contributions, our proposed VideoMogaNet achieves performance on par with or better than state-of-the-art action recognition approaches across multiple video benchmarks (UCF101, HMDB51, Kinetics-400, Diving48), while considerably reducing the cost of the temporal branch.

Many temporal approaches for video recognition rely on explicit cross-channel aggregation projections, which have quadratic complexity in the number of channels, introducing significant computational overhead. In addition, the spatio-temporal representation used for video recognition is biased either toward local order interactions (for CNNs) or toward global order interactions (for transformers). 2026-11-03

ChangeJEPA: Predict Before You Compare for Remote Sensing Change Detection

ChangeJEPA: Predict Before You Compare for Remote Sensing Change Detection

Buddhi Wijenayake、Dhananjaya Jayasundara、Vishal M. Patel

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Remote sensing change detection is typically formulated as Siamese feature comparison, where two images are encoded independently and a decoder determines which discrepancies correspond to meaningful change. This is challenging because the encoders preserve both true scene changes and nuisance variations such as illumination, seasonal, radiometric, and geometric differences. We introduce ChangeJEPA, a bitemporal joint-embedding predictive architecture that reformulates change detection as latent post-state prediction. Given a pre-change image and partial post-change evidence, ChangeJEPA predicts the full post-change representation under two complementary conditions: a prior hypothesis derived from the pre-change scene and an updated hypothesis conditioned on observed post-change context. A joint-embedding predictive loss aligns both hypotheses with the target latent representation, encouraging semantic consistency while suppressing transient appearance variations. Change is then inferred from the disagreement among the pre-change representation, the observed post-change target, and the predicted latent states, capturing complementary evidence of change, prior surprise, contextual update, and prediction reliability. Across SYSU-CD, LEVIR-CD+, and CDD, ChangeJEPA consistently outperforms its decoder matched Siamese baseline by up to 1.51 IoU points and achieves the best IoU and F1-score on all three benchmarks, demonstrating the advantage of latent post-state prediction over direct feature comparison. Page: https://buddhi19.github.io/ChangeJEPA

2026-11-03

Rewrite, Then Veto: Rewriter-Agnostic Edit Gating for LLM-Based Post-OCR Correction

Rewrite, Then Veto: Rewriter-Agnostic Edit Gating for LLM-Based Post-OCR Correction

Jorge Ramón Fonseca Cacho

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

LLMs can repair noisy OCR transcripts of historical documents, but they also change text that was already correct. We propose EditVeto, a veto gate placed after a full-context LLM rewrite. The rewrite is character-aligned to the OCR input, its differences are split into blocks, and a 0.6B-parameter gate keeps or reverts each block. An oracle analysis motivates the design: given the true error locations, per-span generation is worse than no correction, whereas vetoing a full rewrite's edits helps. Trained on rewrites by two Qwen3 models and evaluated, frozen, on the eight HIPE-OCRepair-2026 test sets, the gate improves all five rewriters tested, two of them unseen in training: weighted cMER falls by 0.0026-0.0044 and the preference score rises by 0.15-0.25 (paired bootstrap, p ~ 0.0002 each). On published competition outputs it lowers cMER for three of four teams but raises it for the winning run (0.0050 to 0.0065). In a second, disclosed test look, a retrained gate still improves LoRA-tuned rewriters (cMER -0.0011 to -0.0021), and an ensemble of their gated edits reaches a cMER of 0.0069 and a preference score of 0.928, mostly through fine-tuning and pooling rather than gating. Built only on public models and data, it is behind the winner (0.0050) and tied with the second-ranked run (0.0071), both from the only team using domain pre-training, and ahead of all other published runs. A third, secondary look shows the unchanged gate improving zero-shot Qwen3.8-27B (cMER 0.0114 to 0.0108) but only the preference score of its LoRA-tuned version.

From Activation to Intervention: Interpreting Sparse Visual Features through Reconstruction

From Activation to Intervention: Interpreting Sparse Visual Features through Reconstruction

Jianyang Gu、Jacob Beattie、Mridul Khurana、Julia Chae、Xi Xiao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) decompose visual representations into individual features, offering a promising basis for explaining model behavior. Interpreting these features typically relies on images or pixel regions that most strongly activate them. However, this evidence becomes ambiguous when multiple features with different semantics frequently activate in the same locations, such as texture and color in the same patterns. We propose Reconstructive SAE Intervention (RecSAEI), a framework that shifts feature explanation from passive observation to active visual reconstruction. By intervening on representations along SAE decoder directions and reconstructing the visual output, we provide direct evidence of a feature's semantic meaning. We demonstrate that our method successfully disambiguates feature meanings across multiple datasets and vision encoders. Furthermore, our framework grounds [CLS] SAE interventions in a sparse set of patch SAE decoder directions. Beyond stable target response, this attribution also exposes the compositional relationship between elementary visual patterns and high-level semantics. Our results advocate for intervention reconstruction as an essential complementary signal for explaining neural networks.

Our results advocate for intervention reconstruction as an essential complementary signal for explaining neural networks.

目标检测/分割3D视觉
2026-11-03

SO(3)-Equivariant Learning on CAD Boundary Representations

SO(3)-Equivariant Learning on CAD Boundary Representations

Matteo Ballegeer、Dries F. Benoit

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Learning on boundary representations (B-reps) requires robustness to the pose of a part, since CAD models are not stored in a canonical orientation. Existing B-rep networks either operate on world-frame coordinates and normals, and thus degrade under rotation, or encode each face in a local reference frame. Local frames yield invariance, but reduce inter-face messages to scalars and discard the relative orientation of adjacent faces. We propose SO3brep, an SO(3)-equivariant graph neural network on the B-rep face-adjacency graph. Node features are computed in local frames and mapped into a shared frame by Wigner D-matrices. Clebsch-Gordan tensor products couple these features with the directions between adjacent faces, so that relative orientation is preserved throughout message passing and the output remains SE(3)-invariant. Across three face-segmentation and two classification benchmarks with randomly rotated test parts, SO3brep achieves the highest accuracy among the compared B-rep networks and equivariant point cloud networks, with the fewest parameters. Its advantage is largest with few labeled parts, a setting typical of industrial CAD. Code is available at https://github.com/UGent-CVAMO/so3brep.

多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散视频理解
2026-10-20

When Does Sharing Work? Scaling a Shared SigLIP2 Across Image, Video, Audio, and Audio-Visual Representations

When Does Sharing Work? Scaling a Shared SigLIP2 Across Image, Video, Audio, and Audio-Visual Representations

Xiaofei Sun、Peng Wang、Huayao Liu、Xiaoxiao Xu、Jun Song 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Contrastive pretraining has advanced image-text understanding, yet image, video, and audio encoders are still trained independently, with cross-modal alignment realized mainly through post-hoc binding to a frozen anchor space. We present Omni-Encoder, a general multimodal encoder that processes image, video, audio, and audio-visual inputs within a single SigLIP-2 Vision Transformer with fully shared parameters. Unlike anchor-centric binding or per-modality adapters, all modalities are optimized jointly end-to-end, and the framework is organized from input to training. A modality-native input stage first maps each signal into the shared embedding space, including a 1D-convolutional audio tokenizer that converts mel-spectrograms into tokens aligned with visual patches. A multimodal rotary position embedding parameterized by absolute timestamps then places visual frames and audio segments in a unified spatiotemporal coordinate system. Finally, alternating-modality training interleaves image, video, audio, and temporally aligned audio-visual batches and accumulates their gradients into one update, which we find central to stable joint optimization. Omni-Encoder achieves competitive results on 16 benchmarks across all four modalities with one shared parameter set, and joint training improves each modality over its separately trained counterpart. Code and models will be publicly released.

目标检测/分割医学图像
2026-11-04

Perturbation-Based Reconstruction of Depth-Resolved OCT Attenuation without Requiring a Fully Decayed Signal Tail

Perturbation-Based Reconstruction of Depth-Resolved OCT Attenuation without Requiring a Fully Decayed Signal Tail

Jinglun Yu、Yaning Wang、Shuwen Wei、Jin U. Kang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Depth-resolved attenuation in optical coherence tomography (OCT) can provide quantitative optical contrast beyond structural imaging. However, conventional reconstruction methods can be biased when the OCT signal does not fully decay within the imaging range or when the distal signal tail is dominated by noise or residual reflections from distal sample or support interfaces. Here, we present a perturbation-based Beer-Lambert framework that combines a zeroth-order exponential estimate with a first-order correction, reducing reliance on the unreliable distal signal tail below the current depth. Numerical A-line simulations, Monte Carlo modeling, multilayer silicone-dye-TiO2 phantoms, and ex vivo bovine retina experiments were used to evaluate the method under finite-depth and heterogeneous imaging conditions. The numerical A-line tests showed reduced depth-dependent error against prescribed attenuation profiles; the Monte Carlo comparisons showed less distal drift relative to model-defined benchmarks, while the phantom profiles were compared with separately acquired single-layer OCT calibration values. In a Monte Carlo component test that withheld vessel-mask and vessel-boundary information from reconstruction and used that information only for post hoc evaluation, the first-order correction recovered 61% of the segmentation-informed target vessel contrast and reduced vessel ROI mean absolute error by 65% relative to the fixed zeroth-order baseline. These results suggest that perturbation-based reconstruction may provide a practical approach for depth-resolved reconstruction of effective OCT attenuation when the fully decayed A-line condition is not satisfied.

2026-11-04

SleuthVLM: Do We Really Need Audio-in-Video for Omni Video Understanding?

SleuthVLM: Do We Really Need Audio-in-Video for Omni Video Understanding?

Peiran Wu、Fangfei Gou、Zhaoxi Mu、Changhao Zhang、Jian Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Omni models answer questions about videos by jointly processing sampled frames and audio in one turn, with progress driven largely by scaling model size and omni training data. We ask whether a model needs to hear the audio directly at all, and present SleuthVLM, an agent harness built on a VLM that never hears the audio directly: speech will be transcribed into text, and the VLM will sleuth the video on its own without any predefined workflow. On four omni benchmarks with videos up to 90 minutes, SleuthVLM achieves the best average accuracy: \textbf{75.0%}, 2.6 points above Gemini 3.8 Flash, and it also tops the Video-MME-v2 leaderboard with \textbf{77.8%}; with an open-source 27B VLM, it beats all open-source omni models. We draw three findings: SleuthVLM beats a model that glances; hearing is not the bottleneck, agentic ability is; omni understanding needs better agentic ability, not just more omni data for modality alignment. So we provide a simple but effective starting point for doing omni video understanding. Omni understanding needs better agents, not just bigger models that hear.

生成模型/扩散
2026-11-04

UniRestorer++: Scalable Universal Image Restoration with Multi-Granularity Degradation Modeling

UniRestorer++: Scalable Universal Image Restoration with Multi-Granularity Degradation Modeling

Jingbo Lin、Zhilu Zhang、Fan Li、Renjing Pei、Wenbo Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Universal image restoration aims to handle diverse degradations within a single model, but a reliable scaling strategy remains underexplored. Increasing backbone depth and width can lead to performance saturation, while degradation-aware specialization depends on accurate degradation estimation and is vulnerable to errors on out-of-distribution and real-world inputs. We propose UniRestorer++, a scalable framework based on multi-granularity degradation modeling. It progressively refines degradation groups and introduces group-specific experts, coupling capacity growth with specialization. Degradation-estimation-based routing selects a root-to-leaf path, while granularity-estimation-based routing chooses a level along that path to balance specialization and robustness. Spatially dense degradation representation maps further enable region-aware routing for spatially non-uniform corruptions. We develop CDD-50, a benchmark covering 50 degradation categories from single to penta-degradation. Experiments show approximately log-linear PSNR trends over the evaluated model-size range and stronger scaling gains on out-of-distribution and real-world data than vanilla MoE scaling. UniRestorer++ achieves state-of-the-art performance across benchmarks and demonstrates zero-shot generalization and few-shot transfer on real-world images. Code: https://github.com/mrluin/UniRestorer_plusplus

2026-11-04

Annotator-Free Object Detection via Foundation Model Collaboration

Annotator-Free Object Detection via Foundation Model Collaboration

Yasiru Ranasinghe、Vishal M. Patel

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Object detection research remains constrained by high annotation costs and the closed-set assumptions of supervised models, limiting scalability to novel categories. We present a target-dataset annotation-free object detection framework that eliminates the need for human labeling on the target dataset by leveraging the complementary strengths of pretrained foundation models, including segmentation and vision-language systems, combined with a self-refining training loop. To further reduce reliance on expensive LVLM queries, we introduce a landmark-based clustering mechanism that amortizes labeling across image clusters, improving label consistency while lowering computational cost. Our method autonomously generates segmentation proposals, assigns semantic labels through multimodal reasoning, and iteratively improves both localization and classification using a lightweight open-set detector. This modular pipeline enables adaptation to completely unseen object classes and domains without any human-provided target-dataset annotations, while maintaining compatibility with existing detection architectures. Evaluations on COCO, LVIS, ODinW-35, and Roboflow-100 demonstrate substantial performance gains over state-of-the-art open-vocabulary and long-tail detection methods, achieving 60.8 AP on COCO and 47.4 AP on LVIS under zero-shot settings. Beyond strong benchmark results, our approach provides a scalable foundation for real-world detection systems in unknown environments, where annotation is infeasible.

多模态/VLM3D视觉
2026-11-04

RDT-3D: Resolution-Decoupled Training for High-Fidelity 3D Geometry Generation

RDT-3D: Resolution-Decoupled Training for High-Fidelity 3D Geometry Generation

Jiale Zhang、Yang Liu、Chengyang Li、Jin Yuan、Qianxi Jia 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

High-fidelity 3D geometry generation requires maintaining global structural coherence alongside fine-grained surface details. While recent coarse-to-fine frameworks leverage Rotary Position Embeddings (RoPE) to condition local queries, we reveal a critical training-inference resolution discrepancy: coordinate guidance and geometric supervision are aligned during training, whereas inference decodes high-resolution geometry from coarse spatial coordinates. This mismatch introduces a severe distribution shift that degrades fine details. To overcome this, we propose Resolution-Decoupled Training (RDT), which anchors RoPE guidance at 512 resolution while supervising geometry at 1536, enabling robust detail extrapolation. Additionally, to eliminate high-resolution data bottlenecks, we present Dual3D-300K, a large-scale paired dataset comprising 300K watertight 3D point clouds explicitly aligned across 512 and 1536 resolutions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RDT substantially improves sharp geometric regions, while Dual3D-300K offers a scalable benchmark for high-resolution 3D generation.

While recent coarse-to-fine frameworks leverage Rotary Position Embeddings (RoPE) to condition local queries, we reveal a critical training-inference resolution discrepancy: coordinate guidance and geometric supervision are aligned during training, whereas inference decodes high-resolution geometry from coarse spatial coordinates. This mismatch introduces a severe distribution shift that degrades fine details. To overcome this, we propose Resolution-Decoupled Training (RDT), which anchors RoPE guidance at 512 resolution while supervising geometry at 1536, enabling robust detail extrapolation. Additionally, to eliminate high-resolution data bottlenecks, we present Dual3D-300K, a large-scale paired dataset comprising 300K watertight 3D point clouds explicitly aligned across 512 and 1536 resolutions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RDT substantially improves sharp geometric regions, while Dual3D-300K offers a scalable benchmark for high-resolution 3D generation.

多模态/VLM
2026-11-04

Object-independent evaluation of small-data astronomical image classifiers: architecture, representation, and acquisition shortcuts

Object-independent evaluation of small-data astronomical image classifiers: architecture, representation, and acquisition shortcuts

Eraldo Pereira Marinho

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reported performance in small astronomical image datasets can reflect object reuse, field-of-view differences, acquisition settings, and catalogue-derived properties as well as astronomical morphology. We designed \dataset{} to measure these effects in a four-class task comprising galaxies, globular clusters, nebulae, and open clusters. The dataset contains 370 catalogue-supported physical objects, each represented by three Digitised Sky Survey FITS bands, and uses a group-constrained split of 222 training, 75 validation, and 73 test objects. We compared a custom CNN--Transformer trained from scratch with ImageNet-pretrained ResNet-50 and ViT-B/16 under an adaptive full-field representation, a common angular field, and two central-region-masked controls, using three fixed seeds. In the one-pass object-independent test, mean balanced accuracy on the adaptive view was $0.773\pm0.014$, $0.874\pm0.054$, and $0.866\pm0.023$ for the custom model, ResNet-50, and ViT-B/16, respectively. No primary architecture contrast rejected its null after Holm correction; ResNet-50 and ViT-B/16 differed by 0.007 balanced-accuracy points (95\% cluster-bootstrap interval $[-0.048,0.062]$). Controls based on acquisition and catalogue metadata reached 0.721--0.735, and central-region-masked adaptive views reached 0.731--0.800. These results demonstrate generalisation to unseen physical objects, but also show that image context, acquisition settings, and catalogue-derived object properties carry substantial class information. In this regime, high accuracy cannot be interpreted as evidence that a classifier learned target morphology, and the comparison does not isolate architecture from pretraining.

A Bag of Tricks for Defending Deep Learning-based Face Recognition Pipelines against Backdoor Attacks

A Bag of Tricks for Defending Deep Learning-based Face Recognition Pipelines against Backdoor Attacks

Quentin Le Roux、Marti Jimenez、Yannick Teglia、Philippe Loubet Moundi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The protection of mature, multi-stage Face Recognition Systems is crucial to system providers. This technical report targets the threat model most relevant to industry practitioners: although model training is performed in-house, training data is sourced from possibly-compromised third parties. Given this setting, we present three practical defense "tricks" against data poisoning-based backdoor attacks. The first operates at training-time, detecting poisoned identities during training or prior to a pipeline's deployment. The last two operate at inference-time, filtering suspicious inputs before an answer can be sent back to a potential attacker. Each method is presented with their motivation, algorithms and computational impact, and evaluated on realistic face recognition workloads. Our goal is to provide actionable hardening techniques to system providers.

Our goal is to provide actionable hardening techniques to system providers.

Quo Vadis, Driving Scene Reconstruction? Occlusion-Aware Gaussian Densification

Quo Vadis, Driving Scene Reconstruction? Occlusion-Aware Gaussian Densification

Dung Do Tien、Kien Nguyen Do Trung、Kiet Dang Vu、Trung Thai Tran、Duc Dung Nguyen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

While recent feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting methods enable efficient driving-scene reconstruction from surround-view images, their reliance on a single observation fundamentally hinders novel-view synthesis. Regions occluded in the input can become visible from novel viewpoints, leaving the reconstructed Gaussians under-constrained and incomplete. Moving beyond image-space inpainting for rendered views, we introduce OccSplat, a feed-forward framework whose core contribution is Occlusion-Aware Gaussian Densification, explicitly targeting missing scene geometry rather than missing pixels. By predicting new Gaussian primitives for unsupported regions, OccSplat completes the scene directly in the 3D representation. Specifically, OccSplat first identifies occluded regions that lack Gaussian support through Gaussian Occlusion Mapping. Gaussian Occlusion Completion then generates new primitives for these regions. The predicted primitives are integrated into the original representation to improve novel-view synthesis while retaining feed-forward inference without per-scene optimization. Experiments on the nuScenes benchmark demonstrate that OccSplat improves on the compared methods and yields particularly large gains in occluded regions, leading to more accurate and visually coherent driving-scene reconstruction.

Chronos: Sequential Identity-Preserving Video Generation by Temporal Anchoring

Chronos: Sequential Identity-Preserving Video Generation by Temporal Anchoring

Mengqi Huang、Wenchuan Wang、Zhendong Mao、Yijing Tu、Zhike Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Identity-preserving video generation aims to synthesize high-quality and visually realistic videos from textual and visual conditions while faithfully maintaining the subject's identity across diverse motions. Prior methods have extensively introduced reference-guided control signals and identity-aware conditioning to improve facial alignment from reference images. However, improving static identity alignment alone creates a fundamental tension between identity fidelity and temporal controllability, which frequently causes identity drift or motion suppression under large pose variations and multi-segment sequential actions. To address this, we propose Chronos, a framework that unifies single-segment facial identity preservation and multi-segment sequential action generation under a common temporal formulation. Specifically, Chronos integrates Semantic Prompt Anchoring to adaptively refine the user prompt, Visual Frame Anchoring to transform conditions into visually grounded keyframe anchors for visual guidance, and Metric-Guided Candidate Routing to select the highest-quality videos from heterogeneous generators via multi-dimensional evaluation. Extensive evaluations demonstrate the superiority of our approach, which achieved first place in both Track 1 and Track 2 of the ACM MM 2026 Identity-Preserving Video Generation Challenge, demonstrating its effectiveness across both facial identity preservation and sequential action generation settings.

Extensive evaluations demonstrate the superiority of our approach, which achieved first place in both Track 1 and Track 2 of the ACM MM 2026 Identity-Preserving Video Generation Challenge, demonstrating its effectiveness across both facial identity preservation and sequential action generation settings.

Feature Puzzles: Single-Image Supervision for Multi-View 3D-Aware Segmentation

Feature Puzzles: Single-Image Supervision for Multi-View 3D-Aware Segmentation

Jiahao Ma、Qiang Zhang、Miaomiao Liu、David Ahmedt-Aristizabal、Chuong Nguyen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Humans can easily recognize the same objects across different viewpoints of a 3D scene, whereas teaching models such multi-view-consistent perception typically requires videos or calibrated multi-view captures, which are considerably more expensive and less diverse than large-scale monocular data.~In this paper, we introduce Feature Puzzles, a novel framework for learning multi-view-consistent segmentation using only single-image semantic segmentation supervision. Specifically, we propose a simple yet effective pipeline that constructs multi-view-consistent segmentation pseudo-labels through image cropping and geometric transformations. We further introduce a lightweight, plug-and-play network that leverages the multi-view-consistent image features learned by existing pretrained 3D foundation models and the generated pseudo-labels to perform multi-view-consistent segmentation. Importantly, our flexible framework supports various types of prompts provided from one or multiple views and can be readily extended to open-vocabulary 3D segmentation. Under this single-image supervision, our feed-forward model achieves the best mIoU on NVOS, remains competitive on SPIn-NeRF and ScanNet++ against baselines using per-scene optimization or in-domain multi-view training, and demonstrates zero-shot transfer to open-vocabulary 3D segmentation. Visual comparisons are available on our project page: https://featurepuzzles.github.io/

ReTailor: A Residual Feature Tailoring Framework for Real-Time Cross-Sensor Pansharpening Adaptation

ReTailor: A Residual Feature Tailoring Framework for Real-Time Cross-Sensor Pansharpening Adaptation

Jing-Ze Liang、Wei-Jie Xiao、Zi-Han Cao、Yang-Dong Li、Tian-Yi Hu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Recent pansharpening models achieve strong fusion performance on trained sensors, yet often generalize poorly to unseen sensors. Existing cross-sensor adaptation is limited by target-sensor retraining, which requires target-sensor training pairs and a dedicated training stage, and by per-image zero-shot adaptation, which is computationally expensive due to whole-network optimization for each test image. To address these problems, we propose ReTailor, a real-time sequential cross-sensor adaptation framework. Only a lightweight residual Feature Tailor is rapidly optimized under self-supervised, physics-aware constraints to correct cross-sensor shifts without modifying the well-learned fusion prior. For efficient and effective deployment, ReTailor combines content-aware patch selection, which identifies a few informative patches per image for optimization at negligible cost to improve both quality and efficiency, with rolling residual bootstrap, which inherits sensor-related knowledge across the image stream to further improve quality. Extensive experiments on cross-sensor adaptation settings show that ReTailor mitigates cross-sensor degradation both effectively and efficiently. Moreover, we compare ReTailor with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), a widely used method for fine-tuning, to demonstrate the advantages of our approach. We further evaluate the core principle of ReTailor on two additional remote sensing tasks: super-resolution pansharpening and image segmentation, where it consistently improves performance. In general, ReTailor provides a highly effective and efficient strategy for deploying pansharpening models under real-world cross-sensor degradation, while also offering useful insights into managing cross-sensor degradation in other remote sensing tasks. Code will be released at https://github.com/JingZe-Liang/ReTailor .

2026-11-04

From Pixels to Actions - Towards Native Vision-Language-Action Policies

From Pixels to Actions - Towards Native Vision-Language-Action Policies

Weichen Fan、Haiwen Diao、Penghao Wu、Siyuan Hu、Ziqi Huang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language-action models are typically built on a simple premise: perception is first compressed by visual encoders, and action reasoning begins only afterwards. Yet manipulation often hinges on precisely the visual signals most vulnerable to this abstraction, e.g., local geometry, boundaries, depth, and subtle state changes. This raises a fundamental question: should action policies inherit a fixed visual endpoint, or directly access evolving visual computation? We introduce NEO-VLA, a structurally encoder-free vision-language-action framework. Images are tokenized by lightweight patch convolutional layers and evolve jointly with language throughout the backbone, allowing a flow-matching action expert to draw from instruction-conditioned visual representations at different depths. This simple change reveals a consistent pattern. Native architectures outperform encoder-based counterparts under controlled comparisons; full fine-tuning reduces but does not erase the gap, and additional scale alone cannot recover it. More strikingly, layer-wise probing shows that the action-critical signals often peak well before the final visual representation. Across diverse manipulation settings and distribution shifts, NEO-VLA consequently achieves stronger and more robust control. Together, these results suggest that embodied control benefits from access to visual representations across the computational hierarchy, rather than a fixed encoder output.

2026-11-04

Motion-guided Contrastive Regularization For Latent World Models

Motion-guided Contrastive Regularization For Latent World Models

Yiwen Chen、Qiqi Hou、Jisoo Jeong、Yinhao Zhu、Huaizu Jiang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

JEPA-style world models learn action-conditioned latent dynamics without reconstructing observations, but distributional regularization alone does not ensure that their representations preserve information needed for control. We identify static collapse, a failure mode in which the encoder preserves episode-specific static content while suppressing manipulation-relevant state changes. Because static content is temporally predictable yet diverse across episodes, this failure can persist despite regularizers such as SIGReg. A controlled study on OGBench-Cube shows that introducing randomized backgrounds substantially degrades planning performance while leaving the underlying manipulation task unchanged. To address this problem, we propose motion-guided contrastive regularization. Our approach compares predicted latent transitions with transitions to candidate observations from the same episode, reducing the usefulness of static background identity for distinguishing action outcomes. Optical-flow similarities provide soft contrastive targets, allowing candidates with similar motion to receive similar supervision. Combined with latent prediction and SIGReg, our objective encourages representations to preserve within-episode state distinctions and similarities between related transitions. Our method improves planning success over LeWorldModel (LeWM) from 52% to 78% on OGBench-Cube with randomized backgrounds and from 14% to 54% on LIBERO-Object, while maintaining comparable performance on four other visual-control benchmarks.

CounterFactual Retrieval for Evidence-Aware Individual Animal Identification

CounterFactual Retrieval for Evidence-Aware Individual Animal Identification

Ankit K. Upadhyay、Aleksandr Algasov、Ekaterina Nepovinnykh、Fedor Zolotarev、Tuomas Eerola 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Automated identification of individual animals is moving from small curated studies toward shared benchmarks and reusable foundation models. This progress is useful, but current evaluation leaves the evidence behind a match unresolved, i.e., high retrieval accuracy does not show whether a model used animal features or identity-correlated shortcuts. We address this with Counterfactual Foreground Retrieval (CFR), an evidence-aware evaluation protocol. CFR evaluates the same query-gallery task under different views: full image, foreground-only, and shortcut-only. The full-image measures standard retrieval performance; the foreground-only measures foreground-supported retrieval; and shortcut-only view measure how much identity signal is recoverable from background, or silhouette. This turns a retrieval score into an evidence profile: how well the model performs on the original images, how much of that performance remains when only the animal is visible, and how much identity signal remains recoverable when the animal is removed. We release around 0.5M masks for 34 datasets and code for mask generation and CFR evaluation.

Semantic Residual Vector Quantization for Sign Language Generation

Semantic Residual Vector Quantization for Sign Language Generation

Chang Liu、Ke Han、Niccolò Biondi、Gloria Isotton、Elisa Ricci 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Sign language generation (SLG) maps written sentences into signing motion while preserving both lexical content and fine-grained articulation. This requires a model to decide what signs should be expressed and to realize them through precise handshape, trajectory, facial expression and body coordination. Existing pose-based systems and reconstruction-only motion tokenizers often entangle these two decisions, leaving the generator without an explicit text-aligned intermediate target for what to sign before refining how to sign it. To address this issue, we introduce Semantic Residual Vector Quantization (SRVQ), a framework for SLG that frames generation as a two-level process moving from semantics to kinematics. The newly introduced SRV Quantizer learns a hierarchy in which the first residual level encodes sign content and subsequent levels refine pose, handshape, trajectory, and body coordination, all within a single quantizer and without separate semantic representations or external planning stages. Through word-aware conditioning for hierarchical code generation, a masked semantic generator predicts semantic codes and a kinematic generator completes the residual codes for full SMPL-X motion synthesis. We evaluate using retrieval and FID in a shared text-motion embedding space, alongside standard geometric metrics. On CSL-Daily and Phoenix-2014T, SRVQ achieves R@1 of 82.3 and 70.3, outperforming the previous SOTA by 30 and 46 points, respectively.

2026-11-04

CardioDrift: Automatic Cardiac Motion Tracking on a 3D Atlas Mesh via Shape-aware Coherent Point Drift with Slice Shift Correction

CardioDrift: Automatic Cardiac Motion Tracking on a 3D Atlas Mesh via Shape-aware Coherent Point Drift with Slice Shift Correction

Reza Akbari Movahed、Abuzar Rezaee、Shatha Alamri、Edmond S. L. Ho、Fani Deligianni 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Automatic cardiac motion tracking from cine cardiac magnetic resonance (CMR) imaging is essential for quantifying myocardial motion abnormalities associated with cardiovascular disease. Existing methods fall into two groups: those that represent motion without anatomical correspondence across subjects and therefore limit inter-subject motion assessment; and those that represent motion with globally corresponding points, which typically require a time-consuming and error-prone semi-automatic step to fit a cardiac atlas to the subject space. To address these limitations, this study proposes CardioDrift, a fully automatic, atlas-based probabilistic framework that represents subject-specific cardiac motion on a 3D atlas mesh with globally corresponding points. The Coherent Point Drift (CPD) formulation is generalised to jointly register the atlas surfaces of multiple cardiac regions to the anatomically corresponding contours extracted from cine CMR segmentation maps throughout the cardiac cycle. Slice-shift correction is embedded in the same probabilistic model, estimating slice-wise translations that suppress the misalignment artefact limiting atlas-fitting accuracy. Evaluation on 1301 randomly selected UK Biobank subjects and the M&M dataset, CardioDrift attains Dice similarity coefficients (DSCs) of $92.23\pm8.70\%$, $82.11\pm7.56\%$, and $88.69\pm4.13\%$ for the left ventricle (LV), myocardium (Myo), and right ventricle (RV), exceeding the strongest baseline on every distance and overlap metric of both datasets.

Interactive World Simulation with Cosmos for Physical AI

Interactive World Simulation with Cosmos for Physical AI

NVIDIA、 :、Anis Ahmad、Jon Allen、Malik Aqeel Anwar 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

We present a shared framework that adapts Cosmos world foundation models for real-time interactive world simulation in Physical AI. By streaming visual observations in response to actions and scene controls, the framework enables users and embodied agents to interact with evolving virtual environments. It supports both predicting the visual consequences of actions and generating observations from physical state maintained by an external simulator. We demonstrate this approach across camera navigation and robot manipulation, controllable video transfer, and multi-view autonomous vehicle (AV) simulation. These examples provide a shared foundation for developing interactive virtual environments for Physical AI training and evaluation. Our code and model checkpoints are available at https://github.com/nvidia/cosmos and https://huggingface.co/collections/nvidia/cosmos3-examples. The project website is available at https://research.nvidia.com/labs/cosmos-lab/cosmos-simulation.

We present a shared framework that adapts Cosmos world foundation models for real-time interactive world simulation in Physical AI. By streaming visual observations in response to actions and scene controls, the framework enables users and embodied agents to interact with evolving virtual environments. It supports both predicting the visual consequences of actions and generating observations from physical state maintained by an external simulator. We demonstrate this approach across camera navigation and robot manipulation, controllable video transfer, and multi-view autonomous vehicle (AV) simulation. These examples provide a shared foundation for developing interactive virtual environments for Physical AI training and evaluation. Our code and model checkpoints are available at https://github.com/nvidia/cosmos and https://huggingface.co/collections/nvidia/cosmos3-examples. The project website is available at https://research.nvidia.com/labs/cosmos-lab/cosmos-simulation.

PathoS2VD: From 2D Histopathology to Pseudo Z-Stack Representations

PathoS2VD: From 2D Histopathology to Pseudo Z-Stack Representations

Jiamu Wang、Keunho Byeon、Ji-Uk Kim、Jin Tae Kwak、Heounjeong Go 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

For decades, diagnostic pathology has relied on thin two-dimensional (2D) tissue sections, despite biological tissues exhibiting inherently three-dimensional (3D) morphological organization. Because a single section captures only one focal plane, important morphological cues along the z-axis are inevitably discarded, limiting tissue representation for both human interpretation and computational analysis. In this work, we propose a generative framework that learns z-axis morphological priors from pathology z-stack data, offering a cost-effective alternative to dedicated volumetric imaging, and transfers this knowledge to conventional single-plane histopathology. Specifically, an image-to-video (I2V) latent diffusion model (LDM) is trained to synthesize a pseudo z-stack from a single 2D image, capturing plausible morphological variations across adjacent focal planes rather than reconstructing physical tissue volumes. To enable deployment on large-scale clinical datasets without native z-stack acquisition, we further introduce a prior-preserving mixture fine-tuning strategy that adapts the model to new domains while retaining the learned z-axis morphological prior. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the synthesized pseudo z-stacks exhibit realistic cross-plane morphological continuity and consistently improve downstream prostate cancer classification compared with conventional 2D representations. Our results suggest that learning transferable z-axis priors from limited z-stack data provides a practical way to enrich routine pathology images with complementary morphological information. Code: https://github.com/QuIIL/PathoS2VD.

A 3D foundation model unifying CT interpretation and segmentation

A 3D foundation model unifying CT interpretation and segmentation

Yulu Xie、Zilong Li、Shen Xu、Yifan Bai、Liuhong Shi 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Radiologists interpret computed tomography (CT) scans by jointly determining what a finding represents and where it lies in three-dimensional (3D) anatomy. However, CT models have treated CT interpretation and 3D segmentation as separate tasks, leaving textual findings spatially unverifiable and overlooking potential synergy between clinical understanding and spatial perception. Here we present HoloCT, a generalist 3D foundation model unifying CT interpretation and voxel-level segmentation by jointly modelling whole-volume context and fine-grained anatomical detail, providing 3D masks as evidence. We curated HoloDATA, comprising 195,921 CT scans from 73 public datasets and six clinical cohorts, with 6.2 million instruction dialogues and 2.8 million CT-mask pairs. Across 346 tasks spanning segmentation, lesion screening, visual question answering, and report generation on 61 internal and 12 external datasets, HoloCT outperformed specialist and foundation models. In multicentre validation across 11,455 clinical CT scans from five hospitals, HoloCT outperformed competing models in lesion segmentation and report generation by 18.2 and 15.3 points. It also achieved 85.1% mean accuracy in screening thyroid tumours and colon lesions, exceeding all four participating radiologists in a retrospective reader study. Further analyses revealed bidirectional semantic-spatial synergy: language-guided segmentation delineated unseen anatomical structures and lesions as well as diffuse free-text findings, outperforming the leading approach by 22.1 points, whereas prompting the model to generate corresponding 3D masks alongside its textual outputs significantly improved interpretation performance. HoloCT shows that clinical understanding and spatial perception can be learned together within a single foundation model and offers a path towards more accurate and reliable CT analysis.

HoloCT shows that clinical understanding and spatial perception can be learned together within a single foundation model and offers a path towards more accurate and reliable CT analysis.

2026-11-04

TOP: Transmission-Oriented Remote Sensing Pansharpening

TOP: Transmission-Oriented Remote Sensing Pansharpening

Zi-Han Cao、Jing-Ze Liang、Shao-Jie Wu、Jin-Cheng Di、Liang-Jian Deng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Pansharpening has been extensively studied as a fundamental image fusion task in remote sensing. However, most existing methods focus on improving fusion quality under the implicit assumption that high-resolution observations have already been fully downlinked to the ground. As a result, existing models are highly vulnerable to perturbations introduced by lossy compression used for downlink transmission. To address this limitation, we present, to the best of our knowledge, the first transmission oriented pansharpening (TOP) framework, which unifies compact latent coding, onboard pansharpening, and efficient downlink transmission within a single satellite-toground pipeline. TOP encodes remote-sensing observations into a compact binary latent space, where a lightweight onboard fusion module can efficiently predict the fused binary latent code. Since only the compact fused code is downlinked, the transmission payload is substantially reduced. Overall, TOP enables low-payload downlink and lightweight onboard processing while maintaining high fusion quality under severe bandwidth constraints. Experiments across multispectral and hyperspectral settings show that TOP consistently achieves superior fusion quality at higher compression ratios than representative pansharpening methods paired with standard lossy codecs. For example, on GaoFen-2, TOP attains a fusion PSNR of 31.4 dB at a downlink compression ratio of 50x. This design also points toward transmissionoriented processing for other remote-sensing tasks. The code will be made available at https://github.com/JingZe-Liang/TOP.git.

For example, on GaoFen-2, TOP attains a fusion PSNR of 31.4 dB at a downlink compression ratio of 50x. This design also points toward transmissionoriented processing for other remote-sensing tasks. The code will be made available at https://github.com/JingZe-Liang/TOP.git.

3D视觉
2026-11-04

CrossPressor: Scalable Cross-View Fusion for Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting

CrossPressor: Scalable Cross-View Fusion for Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting

Yuxi Hu、Wenhao Dong、Jun Zhang、Friedrich Fraundorfer

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3D-GS) becomes increasingly difficult to scale to long multi-view sequences. Anchor-based compression methods such as ZPressor alleviate representation growth by decoding Gaussians only from a compact set of anchor feature maps, but keeping these anchors fixed forces each one to aggregate an increasing number of support views. We identify this representation--fusion mismatch and introduce \textbf{CrossPressor}, a training-free method for scalable anchor-based fusion. CrossPressor preserves all decoder-facing anchor queries while constraining the view-growing support sequence through coverage-cycled evidence routing, distributing retained spatial evidence across support views and fusion layers while keeping all support views represented. Routing before K/V projection, together with joint context packing, further reduces support-side fusion cost without retraining. CrossPressor maintains reconstruction quality close to dense fusion while achieving up to $26.12\times$ faster cross-view fusion and $1.43\times$ end-to-end acceleration on long input sequences. Our project page is at: https://yuhsihu.github.io/CrossPressor.

2026-11-04

CAPE-JEPA - Cross-modal Adversarial Probabilistic Embedding JEPA

CAPE-JEPA - Cross-modal Adversarial Probabilistic Embedding JEPA

Aniket Junghare、Biplab Banerjee

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Cross-modal remote sensing image retrieval (RS-CMIR) requires representations that are simultaneously modality-invariant and semantically discriminative, despite the profound physical gap between heterogeneous sensors such as Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) and multispectral imagery. Recent JEPA-based Predictive self-supervised approaches such as X-JEPA formulate cross-modal alignment as a deterministic latent forecasting task, implicitly assuming uniform patch predictability and leaving cross-modal distributional alignment unaddressed. We propose CAPE-JEPA, a probabilistic cross-modal Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture with three contributions: (i) a cross-attentional Gaussian predictor outputting per-patch parameters (mu, sigma^2) conditioned on opposite-modality context, trained via negative log-likelihood to model inherent cross-sensor prediction uncertainty; (ii) adversarial distributional regularization combining unimodal isotropic Gaussian regularization with a novel cross-modal adversarial variant that aligns modality distributions via worst-case Epps-Pulley characteristic function tests without explicit sample pairing; and (iii) in-training contrastive learning with attention-pooled heads for scene-level discriminative grounding. Evaluated on BigEarthNet-14K (BEN-14K) and fMoW across SAR, multispectral, and RGB modalities, CAPE-JEPA surpasses X-JEPA by up to 13.74 points on BEN-14K and 15.59 points on fMoW, outperforming all baselines including SkySense and CROMA across both unimodal and cross-modal directions while using fewer parameters (407.30M vs. 432.72M).

DSRS: Teaching MLLMs to Perceive, Reason, and Segment in Complex Driving Scenes

DSRS: Teaching MLLMs to Perceive, Reason, and Segment in Complex Driving Scenes

Sicen Guo、Zhengfei Song、Kuanhsun Chen、Xu Huang、Ping Zhong 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Existing reasoning segmentation frameworks typically employ multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with broad world knowledge to extract semantics in a human-like manner and generate corresponding segmentation masks. Nevertheless, these frameworks lack the necessary scene-specific awareness. This paper introduces driving scene reasoning segmentation (DSRS), a scene-specific task that requires models to interpret implicit instructions, infer spatial relations, detect potential risks, handle challenging conditions, and comprehend underlying traffic rules, ultimately generating accurate segmentation masks. Meanwhile, we establish a benchmark dataset to facilitate future research on the DSRS task. To pioneer this task, we propose DSRS-Net, a novel architecture with advanced reasoning segmentation capability for automated driving. Specifically, DSRS-Net seamlessly infuses geometric features for deeper reasoning segmentation, without compromising the original multimodal fusion mechanism. This strategy effectively mitigates the modality representation mismatch in MLLMs caused by RGB-only pretraining, while enhancing the integration of geometric, contextual, and linguistic features. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superior performance of DSRS-Net in both driving scene reasoning segmentation and other language-driven segmentation tasks across various public datasets. The source code and dataset will be released upon publication.

PTQ4SAM3: Semantics-Preserving Post-Training Quantization for Segment Anything Model 3

PTQ4SAM3: Semantics-Preserving Post-Training Quantization for Segment Anything Model 3

Xi Xiao、Yunbei Zhang、Lin Zhao、Janet Wang、Xinru Jiang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Segment Anything Model 3 (SAM 3) can segment objects from text prompts and track them across video, but its memory and computation costs make deployment difficult. Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces these costs by using fewer bits. However, a quantized model can select the wrong object or lose track of its target across frames. We call this prompt-alignment drift. Controlled tests show that perturbing low-variance feature directions strongly affects target selection. We propose Semantics-Preserving Quantization (SPQ), which gives more calibration weight to concept-sensitive parameters, preserves residual features, and corrects the mean error in video memory. Fixed text concepts guide calibration without task annotations. All three components operate offline and add no inference operators. With a W4A8 image encoder, SPQ improves RefCOCO Pr@0.5 from 42.1 to 72.5 over PTQ4SAM while retaining 54.8 COCO mask AP. Experiments on LVIS, RefCOCO variants, and SA-V show consistent gains. A CLIP study also shows improved image-text retrieval.

2026-11-04

Semantic Prior-Guided Incomplete Cross-Modal Retrieval with Noisy Labels

Semantic Prior-Guided Incomplete Cross-Modal Retrieval with Noisy Labels

Jiali Chen、Ruitao Pu、Yingke Chen、Dezhong Peng

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Cross-modal retrieval (CMR) aims to bridge the semantic gaps between modalities by learning shared representations. Unfortunately, due to the dynamism and inconsistencies in real-world data acquisition, multimodal datasets frequently encounter severe modality incompleteness. Existing incomplete CMR methods adhere to a label-driven paradigm, relying heavily on categorical annotations as the semantic benchmark. However, label noise is difficult to avoid and often coexists with modality incompleteness. To address this challenge, we propose a novel semantic prior-guided framework for incomplete cross-modal retrieval with noisy labels, termed SPIRN. Specifically, SPIRN uses fixed semantic priors as a consistent reference for robust representation learning, noisy-label handling, and missing-modality completion. Based on this reference, Semantic Priors Guidance (SPG) establishes a stable category-level semantic space, Quality-aware Robust Learning (QRL) dynamically evaluates sample quality to distinguish clean samples from noisy ones and handles noise via reverse learning, Prior-guided Global Label Calibration (PGLC) rectifies corrupted annotations for refined supervision, and Confidence-driven Selective Imputation (CSI) recovers missing modalities from a cross-modal memory bank while filtering unreliable imputations with an increasing threshold. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first framework to jointly tackle the dual challenges of modality incompleteness and label noise in the CMR community. Extensive experiments on four widely used benchmark datasets demonstrate that SPIRN outperforms 11 state-of-the-art methods in various scenarios involving both missing modalities and noisy labels.

XuanJian-DINOSAR: A SAR Foundation Model Based on Global and Local Semantic Consistency

XuanJian-DINOSAR: A SAR Foundation Model Based on Global and Local Semantic Consistency

Chunxiao Wu、Huixin Huang、Jialei Pan、Qiang Liang、Tao Wu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Self-supervised pre-training on synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery enables transferable representations, but the optical-SAR domain gap and speckle perturbations limit representation stability. Most SAR foundation models use masked image modeling (MIM) through pixel reconstruction or structural feature prediction, which is sensitive to speckle or relies on predefined operators. Joint modeling of cross-view semantics and perturbation stability at corresponding spatial locations remains underexplored. We propose XuanJian-DINOSAR, a SAR foundation model based on global and local semantic consistency. We curate over 360K publicly available SAR target chips, detection images, and scene images spanning diverse targets, scenes, and sensors, and adapt an optically pre-trained DINOv2 model to SAR through self-supervised learning. The framework combines DINO global semantic self-distillation, iBOT local masked modeling, and KoLeo feature-distribution regularization to learn globally discriminative and locally structure-aware representations. We introduce a local semantic consistency branch tailored to SAR intensity statistics and coherent imaging characteristics. Shared geometric transformations produce spatially aligned teacher weak views and student strong views. Intensity transformations and Gamma multiplicative speckle perturbations are applied to student views, while a cosine consistency loss aligns patch tokens at corresponding locations, promoting stability under radiometric perturbations. This objective complements the cross-region and cross-scale semantic consistency of DINOv2 multi-crop training, jointly enforcing global and local semantic consistency. Experiments on multiple downstream classification and detection datasets demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on most evaluation metrics, validating the generality and transferability of the learned representations.

Many Pixels, One Composition: Self-Supervised Spectra for Specimen-Level Prediction

Many Pixels, One Composition: Self-Supervised Spectra for Specimen-Level Prediction

Eleftheria Tetoula-Tsonga、George Arvanitakis、Theodoros Giannakas

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Can hyperspectral (HS) imaging be used to predict the elemental composition of physical specimens found on the Earth's surface? We approach this problem as a supervised regression task, using an open dataset of specimens, each comprising a HS cube and elemental characterization. In particular, we train an encoder in a self-supervised fashion that operates on pixel-level; then freeze the encoder and train a lightweight attention-based regression head in a supervised manner, by pairing many representations of a given specimen with its corresponding elemental label. In test specimens, our approach significantly outperforms traditional spectral matching and unmixing baselines. It also outperforms supervised learning models based on the mean spectrum (of similar model capacity), while full model fine-tuning achieves the strongest performance, at substantially higher model capacity.

2026-11-04

Drift-adaptive Representation Preservation for Domain-Incremental Learning

Drift-adaptive Representation Preservation for Domain-Incremental Learning

Hyunmin Kong、Gyutae Oh、Jitae Shin

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pretrained vision models provide strong general-purpose representations, but in Domain-Incremental Learning (DIL), where the input distribution changes sequentially, adapting to new domains often degrades performance on previously learned ones. We observe that forgetting is not uniform across domains and classes and is not explained by training order alone. This motivates us to track each past domain-class concept individually. We use representation drift, defined as the deviation of a past concept's current representation from a fixed anchor, as a measurable surrogate for forgetting risk. The measured drift then serves two complementary roles. A consolidation loss assigns stronger preservation to high-drift pairs, while prototype construction down-weights highly drifted domains to reduce their influence on the class reference. Preservation is thus allocated by measured drift rather than assumed importance. To obtain this signal during training without storing past raw data, we repurpose a Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA). For each domain-class pair, we store only a compact context summary in the backbone feature space and rerun it through the learned context-to-target relation to measure its drift from the fixed anchor. Across DomainNet, Office-Home, CORe50, and CDDB-Hard, our method sustains a strong accuracy-forgetting balance on every benchmark, using substantially less training memory than accuracy-competitive methods and the smallest storage among compared methods.

USI-Brain: Enhancing Underwater Spatial Intelligence via Spatial Data-Driven Vision-Language Modeling

USI-Brain: Enhancing Underwater Spatial Intelligence via Spatial Data-Driven Vision-Language Modeling

Zhiliang Zhu、Zhiqiang Zhao、Xie'an Jiang、Yingxin Lai、Kangye Zheng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Underwater spatial reasoning depends on object boundaries and geometric cues that are obscured by scattering, color distortion, and low visibility. Existing underwater resources capture these cues through images, annotations, and depth maps, but provide fragmented supervision for vision-language models. We present a spatial data engine that connects visual appearance with object-level geometry through enhancement, 2D and geometry-assisted question generation, and expert filtering. It produces 4.27M QA pairs across five capability groups. We use this corpus to train USI-Brain and introduce an Underwater Prior-guided Rectifier (UPR) that applies gated corrections to visual features. USI-Bench evaluates the resulting capabilities with 6,525 held-out QA pairs. On its 15 task metrics, USI-Brain and USI-Brain + UPR improve over Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct by 5.76 and 7.63 points on average, respectively, using signed differences on 100-point scales. Ablations show that geometry-assisted supervision improves depth and ordering by 4.8 and 6.1 points with UPR disabled, while UPR adds 18.0 points in scene description under matched training data. Both variants use raw images at evaluation. Across NautData, UWBench, and MarineEval, USI-Brain achieves the best or joint-best score on 28 of 32 metrics. Model weights and USI-Bench will be released at https://github.com/SKY-Zhu-91/USI-Brain.

2026-11-04

METRIC-GS: Selective Metric-Scale Estimation for 3D Gaussian Splatting

METRIC-GS: Selective Metric-Scale Estimation for 3D Gaussian Splatting

Sooyeun Yang、Jongseong Brad Choi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

A 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) reconstruction inherits arbitrary units from structure-from-motion, so it cannot be measured or used to place AR content until someone supplies a scale. Both standard recipes are fragile on real captures: on five indoor scenes with tape-measured ceilings, a quantile floor-to-ceiling heuristic errs by 27% and metric-depth alignment by 35-71% across three networks. We present METRIC-GS, a selective scale estimator for real indoor photographs: it reads the scale off the reconstruction's own semantic content and, just as importantly, refuses when the evidence is thin. A frozen 2D teacher labels Gaussians by visibility-aware voting; instance-level size priors for architectural and furniture classes (doors, tables, the floor-to-ceiling gap) are fused by a robust maximum-likelihood step; and an inlier-mass test turns thin evidence into an explicit abstention that doubles as a router to a heavyweight metric model. On 47 ScanNet++ scenes with laser ground truth, with a configuration frozen before public evaluation, the estimator alone cuts the heuristic's mean error from 21.1% to 16.6% (9.3% on the 28 scenes it acts on) at ~11 s per scene. MASt3R remains the more accurate standalone estimator (9.9%), but routing only our refusals to it matches running it everywhere with 60% fewer invocations; this selective hybrid is the configuration we advocate. Visibility-aware voting raises the scenes the estimator can act on from 16 to 28 at unchanged deployed accuracy. Applied unchanged to 13 phone and drone captures of our own, on which MASt3R fails its own registration gate 12 times, the system improves all five captures it acts on and degrades none. We also report that a current feed-forward metric model scores 2.3% on a benchmark inside its training mixture and 28-35% on captures it has never seen: a published metric-scale number is a best case, not a deployment estimate.

Intra-Architecture Source Attribution for Stable Diffusion: A New Task with Benchmark Dataset and Baseline

Intra-Architecture Source Attribution for Stable Diffusion: A New Task with Benchmark Dataset and Baseline

Ashutosh Dayal、Mallika Garg、Debashis Sen

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Designing systems that attribute a generated image to its source model has lately become a major area of AI research. Most existing datasets and systems related to image source attribution are targeted towards the detection of architectural signatures of the source models. Distinct from this, focusing on stable diffusion, we introduce a novel task, IntrAA-SD, Intra-Architecture source Attribution for Stable Diffusion, where the source models to be differentiated share the same architectural backbone and differ only due to fine-tuning. The proposed task aligns with the emergence of fine-tuned generators having the same architecture, like Juggernaut XL and RealVis XL, which are fine-tuned versions of Stable Diffusion XL (SDXL). We provide a first-of-its-kind $140$K-image benchmark dataset containing images generated from different fine-tunes of SDXL, along with in-domain and out-of-distribution prompts. We also provide a baseline framework for IntrAA-SD, which leverages a style-conditioned Mixture-of-Experts network. It uses a frozen CLIP backbone to capture the image style and gate the MoE, which processes fine-tuned trace features from a high-resolution LoRA-adapted DINOv2 network. The fine-tuned models used, the prompting strategies considered, and the formation details employed in the novel dataset are elaborated. Our baseline intra-architecture source attribution method is evaluated in terms of attribution accuracy on our dataset, and ablation studies are also performed. Code and dataset is available at https://github.com/mallikagarg/IntrAA-SD.

FedIA: Instance-Aware Cross-Modal Representations for Federated Prompt Learning

FedIA: Instance-Aware Cross-Modal Representations for Federated Prompt Learning

Xuanjing Zhang、Qi Guo、Yanan Qiao、Yong Han、Junge Bo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Federated Prompt Learning (FPL) enables parameter-efficient adaptation of pre-trained Vision-Language Models across decentralized clients without exchanging raw data. To cope with cross-client heterogeneity, existing FPL methods typically pair a globally shared prompt with a client-specific local prompt. However, this design personalizes only at the client level: the same local prompt is applied to all samples of a client, and the global and local prompts are combined in an input-agnostic, fixed manner. Consequently, it overlooks the substantial visual variations among samples within the same client and cannot adjust the balance between shared and personalized knowledge to each input. To address these limitations, we propose Federated Prompt Learning with Instance-Aware Cross-Modal Representations (FedIA), an FPL framework that learns instance-aware cross-modal representations. Specifically, FedIA first uses global text prompts as client-invariant semantic anchors to select image patches most relevant to each candidate class, suppressing distracting regions. The selected visual evidence then conditions the generation of instance-aware local text representations. Finally, an adaptive global-local fusion module calibrates the contributions of local and global text representations for each image-class pair. In this way, FedIA shifts personalization in FPL from the client level to the instance level while preserving the semantic knowledge shared across clients. Extensive experiments under domain shift, pathological label skew, and Dirichlet-based label skew show that FedIA outperforms FPL baselines, with maximum accuracy gains of 1.44\%-4.37\% over the latest baseline across the three settings. Ablation studies and analyses further verify the complementary contributions of instance-aware representation generation and adaptive global-local fusion.

Toward an Optimal Configuration for Tree Species Classification from Bark Images: A Leakage-Safe Ablation Study

Toward an Optimal Configuration for Tree Species Classification from Bark Images: A Leakage-Safe Ablation Study

Maeve Côté

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Background: The automatic identification of tree species from bark photographs is of practical interest for forestry inventory and the wood-processing industry, yet the design choices that govern the accuracy of deep-learning approaches have not been studied systematically, and some prior results may be inflated by leakage of images of the same trunk across training and test partitions. Methods: A systematic ablation study is conducted on the public BarkNet 1.0 dataset (20 species) under the established tree-level, leakage-safe partitioning scheme. Using ConvNeXt-V2 backbones, the following factors are varied in turn and evaluated by five-fold cross-validation: patch size, the strategy by which per-patch predictions are aggregated into an image-level decision, the training regime (single- versus two-stage), backbone capacity, the whole-image baseline, and the number of patches available at inference. Results: Per-patch accuracy rises monotonically with patch size (from 75.6% at 160 px to 89.6% at 512 px), and image-level accuracy rises in step, plateauing near 94.9% around 512 px. Learned aggregation exceeds majority voting by at most 0.7 pp, an advantage that shrinks as patches grow and attention becomes near-uniform. Backbone capacity (pico to tiny) alters accuracy by less than a point, and two-stage training offers no advantage over one-stage. Whole-image classification (91.9%) trails patch-based aggregation, since it admits no bag to average over; accuracy increases with the number of patches at inference, saturating around 64. Conclusions: The study yields a simple recommended configuration: patches as large as possible while leaving enough to average over (around 512 px here), with a nano backbone. It also quantifies the second-order role of learned aggregation and model capacity, providing a reproducible reference point for practitioners and future research

It also quantifies the second-order role of learned aggregation and model capacity, providing a reproducible reference point for practitioners and future research

When can multi-fragment 2D/3D registration be trusted? Calibrated certification and uncertainty-driven view planning for pelvic fracture fluoroscopy

When can multi-fragment 2D/3D registration be trusted? Calibrated certification and uncertainty-driven view planning for pelvic fracture fluoroscopy

Bowen Xiang、Michael I. Miga

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Registering a segmented CT to fluoroscopy can locate every fracture fragment but can guide surgery only when trusted. After each shot, our closed loop registers all fragments, bounds their errors with a calibrated sandwich covariance, flags implausible or ambiguous ones, and stops or plans the next view. On simulated fluoroscopy of 60 held-out fracture CTs with realistic image uncertainty, 95% bounds covered 83% of unflagged fragments and 91% of those within 5mm, but from the second view on 14% failed by over 5mm, a third with bounds of at most 2mm. A fragment's shift when a view was added separated tightly bounded failures best (exploratory; AUC 0.94, bound 0.81). Requiring that the newest view moved no fragment beyond its bound, preferred post hoc, lowered false stops at 2mm from 23% to 6% overall, with 345 instead of 362 correct ones but more trajectories uncertified; delaying the stop did not, and other signals at equal acceptance left 10-15% false. With a segmentation error shared by all views (median Dice 0.89), 21% of this rule's stops were false against 10% without it (15 cases; exploratory). Minimax planning certified sooner than the clinical protocol without the check (4.20 versus 4.72 shots, Holm p <0.001), but no criterion reached the pre-specified endpoint, a true 2mm error, significantly sooner, even in the true basin. On real fluoroscopy of five intact pelves no 2mm certificate was false. Trust takes evidence across views, not only a calibrated local bound.

Role-based Effectiveness of an Epistemic Uncertainty Estimate in Training Models for Semantic Segmentation of 3D LiDAR Point Clouds

Role-based Effectiveness of an Epistemic Uncertainty Estimate in Training Models for Semantic Segmentation of 3D LiDAR Point Clouds

Krishnakumar N、Jaya Sreevalsan-Nair

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Point cloud segmentation is a fundamental task in 3D data analysis, with applications across robotics, augmented and virtual reality, and remote sensing. However, the inherent sparsity, noise, and unordered nature of point clouds introduce significant challenges, particularly in achieving reliable classification. While the state-of-the-art rely on training models for explicit modeling of uncertainty, there is a gap in exploring different roles that estimated uncertainty using generative approaches can play. This work investigates the impact of deterministic uncertainty in point cloud segmentation by explicitly modeling epistemic uncertainty and using it in different roles, namely, as a feature and in loss function. We evaluate a baseline artificial neural network (ANN) and two uncertainty-aware variants (uncertainty as an additional input feature and as a per-sample confidence weight in the training loss) on four large-scale LiDAR benchmarks spanning airborne and mobile acquisition, namely ISPRS Vaihingen, AHN3, Paris-Lille-3D, and Toronto-3D, together with PointNet++ and RandLA-Net references and their uncertainty-augmented counterparts under canonical spatially disjoint train and test splits. Under this protocol, uncertainty-aware modeling improves mean Intersection over Union, recovers minority classes that the baseline ANN never predicts at all, and in half of the configurations improves the alignment between prediction error and estimated uncertainty as measured by Patch Accuracy versus Patch Uncertainty. Our findings indicate that uncertainty can play multiple roles in the segmentation pipeline rather than as a device for improving accuracy alone.

目标检测/分割医学图像遥感
2026-11-03

Lesion Overlap Ranks PET Segmentation Models but Does Not Certify Patient-Level Tumor Burden

Lesion Overlap Ranks PET Segmentation Models but Does Not Certify Patient-Level Tumor Burden

Tushar Kataria、Gerald Sabin、Ponnuswamy Sadayappan、Shireen Y. Elhabian

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Metabolic tumor volume (MTV) summarizes whole-body PET disease burden for prognostic stratification and treatment assessment. Automated MTV is useful only if its estimate is reliable for the individual patient, yet segmentation models are usually evaluated with overlap metrics such as Dice rather than burden error. We examined whether overlap metrics identify unreliable MTV estimates using 1,014 autoPET FDG PET/CT studies. We evaluated LesionTracer predictions held out from fine-tuning, compared five selected released models on 489 tumor-positive patients, and examined the findings in 597 PSMA studies. Across the five FDG models, ranking by higher mean Dice gave the same order as ranking by lower mean relative MTV error ($ρ=-1.00$, exact $p=0.017$). At the patient level, however, out-of-fold Dice tracked relative MTV error strongly ($ρ=-0.75$), yet 82 of 489 patients (16.8\%) fell on opposite sides of the Dice 0.80 and MTV-error 25\% thresholds. Within the training-exposed released models, correlations ranged from $-0.47$ to $-0.74$ over narrower, high-Dice ranges. Out of fold, opposing false-positive and false-negative volumes cancelled about one third of spatial error in net MTV. In 236 of 489 patients, each of the five models missed at least one lesion, though not necessarily the same lesion. Thus, agreement in average model rankings did not establish the reliability of individual MTV estimates or reveal shared lesion misses. PET segmentation benchmarks should report patient-level MTV error and missed-lesion counts alongside Dice and other overlap metrics.

3D视觉
2026-10-28

PointNDF: Cascaded Neural Displacement Fields for Point Cloud Denoising

PointNDF: Cascaded Neural Displacement Fields for Point Cloud Denoising

Michael Y. Wang、Pingping Cai

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Point cloud denoising aims to recover clean geometry from noisy 3D observations while preserving both fine surface details and the underlying spatial distribution of the points. Many learning-based methods adopt a two-stage pipeline that first extracts point-wise features from the noisy input and subsequently predicts point-wise displacements via simple regressions. However, these approaches often lack robust feature guidance and distribution-aware geometric refinement, causing them to fail at maintaining optimal point uniformity and geometric quality. To address these limitations, we propose PointNDF, a denoising framework that combines patch-level geometric context with local displacement prediction. Specifically, a Point Transformer V3 (PTv3) encoder aggregates multiscale geometric information into a global patch descriptor. Rather than directly regressing an unconstrained displacement vector, a Neural Displacement Field (NDF) fuses this global patch descriptor with per-point neighborhood features. It then predicts each displacement through bounded-vector and scale factor branches, thereby achieving a superior, more uniform point distribution. Three cascaded NDF stages propagate local features and progressively refine the point positions. Experiments on PU-Net and ModelNet10 demonstrate competitive denoising accuracy and favorable inference efficiency. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/peridous/PointNDF.
