LongBanana: An Expert-Verified Benchmark for Long-Context Multi-Reference Image Synthesis

LongBanana: An Expert-Verified Benchmark for Long-Context Multi-Reference Image Synthesis

Haoxiang Cao、Yuxuan Zhang、Penghui Du、Bo Li、Jiajiong Cao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We introduce LongBanana, to our knowledge the first expert-designed and expert-verified benchmark for long-context multi-reference image synthesis in the age of agent-driven image generation. The task requires retrieving dispersed visual evidence, rejecting distractors, binding entities and relations, and composing one coherent final image from multiple references. LongBanana contains 90 carefully designed tasks organized by a 3-by-3 taxonomy; each task includes about 32 reference images, 20 constraints, and 50 evaluation items, yielding 4,670 expert-specified atomic evaluation items in total. Its graph-grounded evaluation design instantiates each task with reference images and an entity-relation graph, then converts node, edge, and whole-graph obligations into checklist items for Content Fidelity, Relational Binding, and Global Constraint Satisfaction. Reviewed expert labels include a 15% double-labeled subset with 89.4% exact agreement and 0.83 quadratic weighted kappa. We further introduce LongBanana-Gen, an agent-harness framework for long-horizon image generation; experiments show that minimal agent harnesses improve over direct generation, while a ReAct-Plan-Exec-Reflect (RPR) workflow yields harness-dependent gains by strengthening evidence grounding and repair. Together, LongBanana provides an auditable testbed for measuring dense visual evidence use and a shared target for developing stronger agent-driven image-generation systems. Code and data are available at the project page: https://long-banana.com/.

Route First, See Later: Enabling Vision Agents to Reason Scientifically by Separating Routing Policy from Action Spaces

Route First, See Later: Enabling Vision Agents to Reason Scientifically by Separating Routing Policy from Action Spaces

Shubhabrata Mukherjee、Jeffrey Donatelli、Daniela Ushizima

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Scientific image-analysis workflows require agents to select among diverse capabilities like description, detection, counting, and segmentation. Current multi-tool architectures conflate intent interpretation with visual execution, masking whether a failure arises from flawed reasoning (misrouting) or failed perception (invalid tool execution). This lack of auditability renders agents unreliable for high-stakes scientific automation. We introduce ZETA, an extensible architecture that operationalizes the decoupling of routing policy from action execution. ZETA resolves scientific intent before invoking specialized visual tools, treating measurement as a deterministic operation grounded in explicit geometry rather than generative output. This architecture exposes the "black box" of agent decision-making, allowing routing correctness and downstream execution to be evaluated as independent failure modes. To our knowledge, we provide the first controlled study in scientific vision-agent routing that isolates explicit routing-policy specification while holding the legal action interface fixed. Evaluation on a zero-shot benchmark of 51 scientific-intent queries shows that explicit routing semantics can substantially improve routing accuracy, particularly at semantically adjacent action boundaries, although improvements vary across models. For Qwen 3.8, explicit routing-policy specification improves segmentation routing accuracy from 6.2% to 75.0% and detection routing accuracy from 60.0% to 100%. ZETA's execution traces make routing decisions and downstream artifacts separately observable, providing a basis for distinguishing intent-selection errors from visual execution failures. ZETA establishes the necessary infrastructure to isolate execution errors from interpretive ones, a critical requirement for deploying reliable scientific vision agents.

2026-11-04

EvoWorld: Towards Out-of-Sight World Evolution in Generative Video Models

EvoWorld: Towards Out-of-Sight World Evolution in Generative Video Models

Yequan Bie、Dengyang Jiang、Zhixuan Chen、Xiaozhuan Liang、Haoxuan Che 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generative video models are increasingly used as visual world simulators, yet their primary strength lies in rendering plausible pixels rather than reasoning about how a world should evolve. Coherent world evolution requires more than visual fidelity: when an event occurs, a model must infer its consequences for the entities in the world and then render the resulting state. For example, after a wild beast appears, the model should not only depict the wild beast, but also infer whether a nearby person should flee, take cover, or fight back based on the realized scene. Existing diffusion-based video models lack an explicit mechanism for maintaining world state and reasoning over such event-conditioned consequences, often producing visually plausible but behaviorally static or causally inconsistent outputs. To make this reasoning gap concrete and measurable, we propose a new problem, Out-of-Sight Causal Evolution, which requires a model to render an event, reason about its consequences for the evolving world state, and reveal a causally plausible outcome as the world continues to evolve, even out of sight. As a baseline reference solution, we introduce EvoWorld, a brain-renderer collaboration framework in which a vision-language model serves as the world brain: it observes generated segments, maintains an evolving account of the world, and reasons about plausible state transitions, while a segment-autoregressive video renderer realizes them visually. We further introduce EvoWorld-Data and EvoWorld-Bench for training and evaluating event realization, causal reactions, and revisit consistency. Experiments show that EvoWorld substantially improves event-conditioned causal evolution over recent models while preserving visual quality and consistency, establishing a reference point for reasoning-guided, autonomous world evolution beyond the current field of view.

Generative video models are increasingly used as visual world simulators, yet their primary strength lies in rendering plausible pixels rather than reasoning about how a world should evolve. Coherent world evolution requires more than visual fidelity: when an event occurs, a model must infer its consequences for the entities in the world and then render the resulting state. For example, after a wild beast appears, the model should not only depict the wild beast, but also infer whether a nearby person should flee, take cover, or fight back based on the realized scene. We further introduce EvoWorld-Data and EvoWorld-Bench for training and evaluating event realization, causal reactions, and revisit consistency. Experiments show that EvoWorld substantially improves event-conditioned causal evolution over recent models while preserving visual quality and consistency, establishing a reference point for reasoning-guided, autonomous world evolution beyond the current field of view.

2026-11-04

TailSteer: Trajectory-Tail Steering for Drift-Resistant Multi-turn Image Editing

TailSteer: Trajectory-Tail Steering for Drift-Resistant Multi-turn Image Editing

Ruikang Li、Zixuan Chen、Jiarui Wu、Mingde Yao、Yiyuan Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Strong single-turn editing performance does not guarantee reliable multi-turn interaction: as edited outputs are repeatedly reused, small unintended changes can accumulate into severe visual drift. This long-term drift is often mitigated through training on multi-turn data or cross-turn rollouts, potentially altering pretrained priors, or through specialized inference-time mechanisms that are difficult to scale to foundation models. We introduce TailSteer, a final-stage training framework that improves multi-turn robustness while retaining the generative and editing capabilities acquired through earlier training. TailSteer follows a simple principle: keep early denoising dynamics close to those of the base model and concentrate preservation corrections near the trajectory tail, where fine-grained deviations can be suppressed. These corrections are learned from the editor's own on-policy denoising rollouts, using unchanged source content as a preservation reference without requiring curated ground-truth editing pairs. Experiments across the FLUX, SenseNova, and Qwen families show that TailSteer substantially reduces cumulative drift over 15-20 editing turns while largely preserving base-model performance on extensive text-to-image and single-image editing benchmarks. These results demonstrate that long-horizon editing robustness can be unlocked in foundation models after TailSteer, rather than by relearning their existing capabilities.

2026-11-04

CLEAR: Color-Texture Latent Alignment for Single Image Reflection Removal

CLEAR: Color-Texture Latent Alignment for Single Image Reflection Removal

Tu Vo、Sheir A. Zaheer、Chan Y. Park

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Single Image Reflection Removal (SIRR) seeks to recover the transmitted scene from a photograph captured through glass. Recent latent restoration methods use pretrained generative priors, but many recover detail by feeding high-frequency features from the degraded input back into the decoder. This works for degradations such as blur, noise, or haze, where input detail mostly belongs to the scene. It is less suitable for SIRR, where reflections add a second structured layer: directly reusing the input's skip connections can sharpen the image while also bringing reflection edges back. Latent compression also introduces color drift and fine-detail loss. We propose CLEAR (Color-Texture LatEnt Alignment for Single Image Reflection Removal), a deterministic, single-timestep, non-iterative latent framework that uses a pretrained latent diffusion backbone as a natural-image prior. A physics-guided separator organizes the prediction around the transmission, reflection, and residue formation model and outputs coarse layer latents in one forward pass. The Hybrid Feature Alignment Module (HFAM) then treats input-derived skips as contaminated layer-mixture features. It rectifies their high-frequency content with a clean-transmission-aligned color query, learned from the input features themselves under ground-truth feature supervision, keeping transmission-consistent texture while attenuating reflection-colored detail. CLEAR achieves the best average across fidelity and perceptual metrics on six real-world benchmarks and the lowest LPIPS on every dataset, with the largest gains on the hardest benchmarks.

2026-11-03

GeoDIVE: Geospatial Descent Informed by Value Estimation

GeoDIVE: Geospatial Descent Informed by Value Estimation

Hamza Iqbal、Dan Cher、Eric Xing、Pranavi Kolouju、Abby Stylianou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Finding specific objects, structures, and spatial patterns supports critical work in agriculture, infrastructure planning, environmental monitoring, and disaster response. These features, however, are often dispersed across very large regions and span dramatically different spatial scales, which defines a search space that is costly to embed, maintain, and search naively. We introduce GeoDIVE, a model that treats a multiresolution satellite image gallery as a forest and uses best-first traversal to determine which tiles to encode. At each visited node, a lightweight model then predicts an optimistic estimate of the best query similarity hidden among its descendants using the query and subtree representations. In evaluations with image and open-vocabulary text queries, GeoDIVE matches exhaustive search at 10% of the encoding cost for image queries and leads all tested baselines at every budget in both modalities. To support future evaluation of multiscale geospatial search, we also release object-level image and text queries for Git-10M, constructed from Microsoft building footprints and OpenStreetMap polygons with VLM-generated captions. Source code and models will be made publicly available.

Anchored Slice Propagation with Boundary Transport for 3D CT Segmentation from Three Orthogonal Annotated Slices

Anchored Slice Propagation with Boundary Transport for 3D CT Segmentation from Three Orthogonal Annotated Slices

Tushar Kataria、Md Rakibul Haque、Shireen Y. Elhabian

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Sli2Vol propagates a binary mask through affinities between adjacent slices, but an error can persist because each prediction becomes the input to the next step. Three orthogonal annotations reduce dependence on any one chain, yet they offer more than three starting masks: where a chain crosses another annotated plane, the correct label is already known. Those labels could correct drift during propagation and indicate how much to trust each chain at fusion. Between annotated planes, the information carried by the chain may matter as well. A binary mask records foreground membership but discards distance to the boundary, a cue that could help follow gradual shape changes between slices.We address these two sources of error with a fixed pretrained affinity backbone. We anchor each chain by correcting its prediction where it crosses another annotated plane, then use its agreement with those labels to weight fusion. We compare this anchored mask with two ways of transporting boundary geometry: a truncated signed-distance field (SDF) propagated by dense affinity averaging, and a contour whose vertices follow motion cues from the same affinity. The contour tracker starts from a hand-crafted rule and learns a motion correction from supervoxel pseudo-objects in 506 CT volumes, without manual organ labels. On 2,091 held-out structure instances from approximately 403 CT scans spanning 17 abdominal structures, anchoring raises mean Dice from 0.737 with orthogonal mask propagation to 0.769 and improves every structure. Beyond the anchored mask, learned contour tracking improves Dice by 5.7\% for the esophagus and 5.9\% for the postcava, while SDF transport improves right-adrenal Dice by 2.8\%. The anchored methods have similar mean Dice (0.769--0.772); contour tracking performs worse on the kidneys, stomach, and hepatic vessel tree.

GaussianRenderFormer: Transformer-based Rendering of 3D Gaussian Splats

GaussianRenderFormer: Transformer-based Rendering of 3D Gaussian Splats

Shahaf Valencia Levy、Sagie Benaim

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Can a neural network learn to render 3D Gaussian splats on its own, with no rasterizer in the renderer? We present GaussianRenderFormer, a transformer that takes a set of Gaussians and a camera and outputs the image directly. Each Gaussian becomes one token in a view-independent encoder, and a view-dependent decoder turns small patches of camera rays into pixels by attending to those tokens. Trained on 26,820 Objaverse objects pruned to 20,000 Gaussians each, GaussianRenderFormer renders 300 unseen objects at 38.08 dB PSNR against the full, unpruned splats, while rasterizing the same pruned input reaches 44.02 dB. The gap is not one of generalization, and our diagnostics are consistent with a bottleneck in the view decoder: telling it the projected footprint of each Gaussian, which it could in principle infer, adds 1.4 dB. Under-training is not ruled out. Code, trained models and videos: https://svlwoof.github.io/gaussianrenderformer

Multimodal Fusion Methods for Whole-Slide Images and Clinical Data in Inflammatory Bowel Disease

Multimodal Fusion Methods for Whole-Slide Images and Clinical Data in Inflammatory Bowel Disease

Ankana Banerjee、Mark van der Giezen、Ole Gunnar Aasprong、Emiel A. M. Janssen、Øyvind Steinsbø 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal AI fusion of histology and clinical data is an active research area in computational pathology, but fusion methods are rarely compared under identical conditions and across different clinical endpoints. We present a modular framework in which slide-level embeddings, obtained by training an attention-based aggregator over patch embeddings from a frozen foundation model, and per-visit clinical embeddings are fed into 15 interchangeable fusion methods with a shared output dimension and prediction head (except decision-level methods). Using a prospective cohort of newly diagnosed inflammatory bowel disease patients, we predicted STRIDE-II-aligned remission outcomes from baseline or follow-up visits. The best fusion method reached macro-F1 of 0.57-0.75, outperforming both single-modality models in four of six scenarios, and differed by outcome and input. Image reliance, the drop in balanced accuracy when the slide embedding is removed, tended to follow the remission type - highest for histological and lowest for endoscopic remission; methods with similar accuracy relied on the slide to very different degrees.

How Finely Can Vision-Language Models See Biology? Bio-VQA: A Benchmark for Taxonomic, Morphological, Spatial and Individual Reasoning

How Finely Can Vision-Language Models See Biology? Bio-VQA: A Benchmark for Taxonomic, Morphological, Spatial and Individual Reasoning

S M Rayeed、Qian Ma、Charles V. Stewart、Yao Ma

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In biodiversity monitoring, a single image raises several questions: which taxon, how many, which trait, and whether the individual has been seen before. Vision-language models (VLMs) could answer all of these from the image, but their ability is largely untested in biology, as existing benchmarks focus mostly on species identification within a few taxonomic groups. We present Bio-VQA, the largest biological visual question answering (VQA) benchmark and the only one to span four aspects of biological visual understanding: hierarchical taxonomic reasoning, fine-grained attribute grounding, quantitative spatial reasoning, and individual re-identification. Bio-VQA contains 1.86M questions over 217K images from 11 public datasets, and extends right-or-wrong scoring to graded scoring, so a near-miss counts for more than a distant one. Evaluating 18 open-weight models from 3.75B to 38.4B parameters, we find that general visual capabilities do not transfer to biological organisms: the best model counts animals exactly on 41.7% of images with 2 to 10 individuals, less than half its accuracy on everyday objects at the same counts. Taxonomic accuracy falls to one sixth, from 0.72 at phylum to 0.12 at scientific name, and scaling helps mostly at coarse ranks. The gap to a domain specialist widens accordingly, from 0.731 against BioCLIP-2's 0.874 at class (1.2x) to 0.269 against 0.544 at scientific name (2x). On trait size measurement, 15 of the 18 models do no better than an image-blind baseline that predicts one fixed number for every specimen, and on re-identification, 17 of the 18 models fall below chance under adversarial conditions. These failures persist across a tenfold range of model size. We hope Bio-VQA becomes a standard test of how well VLM capabilities transfer to the biological domain.

FedXCrop: Privacy-Preserving Crop Disease Detection via Federated Learning with Post-Aggregation Explainability under Non-IID Farm Distributions

FedXCrop: Privacy-Preserving Crop Disease Detection via Federated Learning with Post-Aggregation Explainability under Non-IID Farm Distributions

Kriti Saini、Manikandan P

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Deep learning can classify crop diseases accurately from benchmark leaf images, but centralized training requires farms to share raw data, and predictions without explanations offer limited support for treatment decisions. We present FedXCrop, a federated crop disease classification pipeline that quantitatively evaluates explanations of the aggregated global model. A MobileNetV2 model trained centrally on PlantVillage achieved 99.38% test accuracy (standard deviation 0.05 over three seeds) on a fixed test set of 5,428 images across 38 classes. Federated models initialized with ImageNet weights were trained for 30 rounds across five clients. FedAvg achieved 98.81% accuracy with identically distributed clients and 98.81%, 98.59%, and 97.78% under Dirichlet label skew (alpha = 1, 0.5, and 0.1, respectively). FedProx was not significantly different from FedAvg at any heterogeneity level (McNemar p > 0.3). Grad-CAM and SmoothGrad explanations were evaluated on 380 test images. Both attributed more energy to the leaf than chance (0.70-0.78 versus 0.60), while Grad-CAM maps from federated and centralized models showed Spearman correlations of 0.74-0.76. Agreement decreased as heterogeneity increased, lesion localization was only modestly above chance, and a model-randomization test indicated that SmoothGrad maps depended substantially on image structure rather than learned parameters. In this setting, label skew reduced federated accuracy by 0.6-1.6 percentage points relative to centralized training. FedAvg largely preserved Grad-CAM behavior, while FedProx provided no measurable benefit.

BiCG-Net: Target-Specific Image Quality Assessment via Observation Modeling and In-Image Differencing

BiCG-Net: Target-Specific Image Quality Assessment via Observation Modeling and In-Image Differencing

Yibu Yang、Tingqiong Cui、Liming Lei、Hao Xiong、Jun Xu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We estimate the quality of a specific target region (e.g., a train body) in an image, rather than the whole image. This differs fundamentally from whole-image No-Reference Image Quality Assessment (NR-IQA): when the target occupies a small fraction of the frame, the whole-image score is dominated by background, and no public benchmark provides target-level human Mean Opinion Score (MOS). We estimate target quality via nested sampling - a sequence of patches expanding outward from the target box - and infer quality from the relative structure of this sequence. This motivates BiCG-Net, a student network that distills an 11-teacher ensemble through a linear observation model and an in-image differencing mechanism, which provably cancels the target term and teacher-specific bias. We train and test the student independently on each dataset, and evaluate zero-shot transfer across benchmarks. On CSIQ and LIVEC, the student reaches PLCCs of 0.9009 and 0.9133 against human MOS, exceeding the best single teacher and the teacher consensus. On a self-built MOS-free railway dataset, the student reaches a PLCC of 0.9855 against a weak label. The student exceeds its teachers because it learns a new mapping from the relative structure of teacher readings to the ground-truth label, rather than mimicking them. Residual analysis on CSIQ and LIVEC shows the prediction residual is significantly correlated with background contamination, teacher dispersion, and label extremity, demonstrating the student extracts information the consensus does not contain. Under CSIQ-to-LIVEC transfer, the frozen student reaches a PLCC of 0.8416, exceeding the consensus by 0.1947. We position BiCG-Net as an offline target-level quality auditing and label-generation engine; its 39,482-parameter student is compact by design, acting as a unifying layer mapping heterogeneous teacher readings onto a common quality scale.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉机器人视觉
2026-11-04

Rohbau3D-Semantics: Dense Semantic and Instance Labels for Point Clouds of Indoor Shell Construction Scenes

Rohbau3D-Semantics: Dense Semantic and Instance Labels for Point Clouds of Indoor Shell Construction Scenes

Lukas Rauch、Thomas Braml

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

We present the Rohbau3D-Semantics dataset, which adds dense semantic and instance-level labels to laser scans of the previously released Rohbau3D domain data from shell construction sites. The dataset comprises 504 point cloud scenes from 14 survey sites, each captured with a terrestrial laser scanner and stored in a scanner-centered local coordinate system. Unlike point clouds sampled from reconstructed meshes or synthetic building models, the data preserve the characteristic properties of real laser scanning, including non-uniform sampling, measurement artifacts, reflections, and fine geometric detail. Combined with manual labels, expert oversight during pointwise annotation, and full-scene review, this yields a high-quality benchmark for semantic scene understanding in complex built environments. The dataset includes 17 class labels of building components and one background class, along with instance annotations for object-level analysis. In addition to the 3D point clouds, the release also provides scripts for deriving geometrically aligned panoramic and cube-map representations of color, LiDAR reflection intensity, depth, surface normals, semantic labels, and instance labels from the 3D arrays. We describe the acquisition pipeline, annotation protocol, quality-control process, and panorama-generation procedure, and provide benchmark splits and baseline experiments for semantic segmentation. By combining precise terrestrial laser scans, dense annotations, and a wide variety of shell-construction scenes, Rohbau3D-Semantics bridges the gap among synthetic indoor datasets, mesh-derived benchmarks, and smaller construction-specific resources. The dataset is intended to support research on 3D scene understanding, construction-site analytics, and multimodal scene analysis in the built environment. Access and download the full dataset via https://github.com/RauchLukas/rohbau3d

The dataset comprises 504 point cloud scenes from 14 survey sites, each captured with a terrestrial laser scanner and stored in a scanner-centered local coordinate system. Unlike point clouds sampled from reconstructed meshes or synthetic building models, the data preserve the characteristic properties of real laser scanning, including non-uniform sampling, measurement artifacts, reflections, and fine geometric detail. Combined with manual labels, expert oversight during pointwise annotation, and full-scene review, this yields a high-quality benchmark for semantic scene understanding in complex built environments. The dataset includes 17 class labels of building components and one background class, along with instance annotations for object-level analysis. In addition to the 3D point clouds, the release also provides scripts for deriving geometrically aligned panoramic and cube-map representations of color, LiDAR reflection intensity, depth, surface normals, semantic labels, and instance labels from the 3D arrays. We describe the acquisition pipeline, annotation protocol, quality-control process, and panorama-generation procedure, and provide benchmark splits and baseline experiments for semantic segmentation. By combining precise terrestrial laser scans, dense annotations, and a wide variety of shell-construction scenes, Rohbau3D-Semantics bridges the gap among synthetic indoor datasets, mesh-derived benchmarks, and smaller construction-specific resources. The dataset is intended to support research on 3D scene understanding, construction-site analytics, and multimodal scene analysis in the built environment. Access and download the full dataset via https://github.com/RauchLukas/rohbau3d

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉
2026-11-04

Reasoning-Aware Cross-Modal Association Unlearning in Multimodal Large Reasoning Models

Reasoning-Aware Cross-Modal Association Unlearning in Multimodal Large Reasoning Models

Xinghao Li、Chenchen Tan、Youyang Qu、Shujie Cui、Xingliang Yuan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal large reasoning models (MLRMs) integrate visual inputs with textual and parametric knowledge, allowing sensitive information to be accessed through cross-modal associations during reasoning. This raises privacy concerns when a visual input should no longer provide access to such information. Machine unlearning can remove this influence without retraining the model from scratch. However, multimodal unlearning methods mainly target samples, concepts, or knowledge content, without separating the knowledge from the cross-modal association through which it is accessed. In reasoning models, target information may disappear from the final answer while remaining in intermediate reasoning, or be replaced by incorrect substitute knowledge. We therefore study cross-modal association unlearning, where the forget target is a designated visual-to-knowledge association: the model should suppress target-bearing behavior under the designated visual context while preserving access to the same knowledge in permitted contexts. We propose Visual-Keyed Counterfactual Rebinding (VKCR), a parameter-efficient framework for cross-modal association editing. VKCR first localizes the edit to a compact association-conditioned parameter subspace. It then reduces support for the designated target at task outputs and counterfactually rebinds the affected reasoning behavior by discouraging target-bearing or unsupported continuations while favoring valid non-sensitive alternatives. We further introduce CLEAR-R and MLLMU-R to evaluate target leakage, hallucinated reassociation, reasoning preservation, and modality-specific utility. Experiments show that VKCR substantially reduces image-conditioned target leakage and hallucinated reassociation, including across heterogeneous task formulations, while preserving non-sensitive visual reasoning and permitted text-only knowledge.

MotoVerse: A Benchmark for Predictive Perception of Motorized Two-Wheelers in Dense Unstructured Traffic

MotoVerse: A Benchmark for Predictive Perception of Motorized Two-Wheelers in Dense Unstructured Traffic

Varun A. Paturkar、Shankar Gangisetty、C. V. Jawahar

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Motorized Two-Wheelers (MTWs) dominate personal transport across much of the Global South, yet remain largely overlooked in predictive perception research for autonomous driving and ADAS. We introduce MotoVerse, a benchmark for predictive perception from an MTW's ego-centric perspective. MotoVerse comprises 949 maneuver clips collected in dense, unstructured traffic and trajectory annotations for over 31,000 surrounding road agents, including over 16,000 MTWs, enabling two complementary tasks: Ego-Rider Maneuver Anticipation (ERMA) and Traffic Agent Trajectory Prediction (TATP). Each clip is further annotated with an MTW-specific challenge taxonomy comprising dynamic ego-motion, weak traffic structure and contextual occlusion, enabling challenge-aware evaluation. We benchmark established conventional prediction models, general-purpose Vision-Language Models (VLMs), driving-specific VLMs and vision-language-action models. Our experiments reveal three key insights: (i) video backbones reach most of their full-observation accuracy from the first fifth of a maneuver, whereas zero-shot VLMs need most of the maneuver to be observed; (ii) zero-shot VLMs, whether general-purpose or driving-specific, largely remain close to simple baselines on both tasks; a preliminary LoRA study shows that adaptation lifts anticipation sharply but leaves trajectory prediction behind conventional models; and (iii) the rider's own motion drives forecasting difficulty: for every model, including a static baseline, errors are highest during left turns and rise under dynamic ego-motion. We believe MotoVerse will serve as a challenging benchmark for safer and more robust predictive AI for intelligent two-wheelers. The dataset and evaluation code are publicly available at https://github.com/VarunIIITH/MotoVerse.

CoDecide: Content-Adaptive Video Decoding for High-Throughput Video Analytics

CoDecide: Content-Adaptive Video Decoding for High-Throughput Video Analytics

Shristy Shah、Thomas B. Moeslund、Kamal Nasrollahi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Analytics on video archives are bottlenecked by decoding. In a dense pipeline that runs a detector on every frame, decoding is a small fraction of the runtime, but once the detector is restricted to selected frames, decoding takes over as the main cost. We introduce CoDecide, a retrospective analytics system that only decodes the frames where new content appears, using signals from the compressed bitstream to find them. Motion tells us something is moving, but not whether it is new to the scene or has been there the whole time. To make that distinction, we supervise a lightweight compressed-domain student on a label-free novelty signal from a frozen DINOv2 teacher, which fires on semantically new regions rather than on any motion. Consequently, the student model generalizes to new camera deployments without requiring per-camera retraining. At inference, it decides which frames to decode and which regions to reconstruct. Detections from the decoded frames are propagated to the skipped frames using motion vectors. We evaluate CoDecide with a leave-one-dataset-out protocol on VIRAT, MEVA, and ShanghaiTech. Against a dense per-frame detector, CoDecide recovers more moving-object detections than prior compressed-domain selectors at an equivalent decoding budget, outperforming even a competitor trained directly on the target camera. CoDecide runs 15 to 24x faster than a pipeline that decodes and detects every frame. Code is available at https://github.com/shristy51/CoDecide.

Beyond Infraction Scores: Failure-Boundary Profiles for Closed-Loop Evaluation of Driving and Robot Navigation Agents

Beyond Infraction Scores: Failure-Boundary Profiles for Closed-Loop Evaluation of Driving and Robot Navigation Agents

Milan Bulat、Gino Ardelean、Ellington Kirby

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Closed-loop driving benchmarks in CARLA rank agents by a driving score that multiplies route completion by an infraction penalty. This score does not distinguish an agent that stops in front of an obstacle from one that pushes past it. In a corridor closed by two parked cars, for example, one agent that drives through with contact averages 52.2 on the blocked widths, while the agents that stop average between 37.8 and 40.6. We propose failure-boundary profiles, an evaluation in which each scenario family varies one measured factor, such as corridor width or the time gap in oncoming traffic, and each variant is certified from measurement as one where the agent should proceed or one where it should stop. Along this axis we estimate two curves per agent, commitment (the probability of attempting the passage) and competence (the probability of passing without contact), and locate where each crosses one half, with intervals that account for the simulator's run-to-run variance. The certified labels also give a summary metric, decision accuracy, which credits clean passes on passable variants and stops on blocked ones. We evaluate seven driving agents on seven CARLA families. The rule-based expert reaches a decision accuracy of 0.74 and TFv6, the highest-scoring learned agent, 0.60. Agents with similar driving scores fail in different ways, and agents that reliably stop at a blocked corridor still rarely wait for a gap in oncoming traffic. We also apply the method, unchanged, to a warehouse robot in Isaac Sim. The navigation policy we test stops at blocked gaps in only one of three families, and two fine-tuned versions of it pass more gaps cleanly but no longer stop at all.

AgentCount: An Agentic Workflow for Traceable Colony Counting and Insight Generation from Agar Plate Images

AgentCount: An Agentic Workflow for Traceable Colony Counting and Insight Generation from Agar Plate Images

Zhuosheng Liu、Austin E. Weber

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Colony counting is commonly used to evaluate microbial populations in food safety testing and microbiological studies. Despite its wide application, manual counting is time-consuming and operator-dependent, and the final count often lacks a record of the objects included in the measurement. To address these limitations, this study presents AgentCount, an open-source agentic workflow for colony counting and result interpretation from agar plate images. Plate photographs and plating metadata are processed through seven agents for image quality control, plate localisation, colony detection, counting, CFU calculation, validation, and reporting. Colony detection is performed using Segment Anything Model 3 (SAM 3) with multiple text prompts and morphology-based filtering. After that, replicate statistics are calculated and a large language model (LLM)-based agent is used to generate a results narrative. Intermediate outputs, including quality scores, segmentation masks, detections, and warnings, are retained to support review of the reported values. The workflow also extends to skill-guided analysis of archived microbial population data. In a retrospective comparison with two previously published studies, we benchmarked 241 published endpoints, including all 216 mean +- SD values of the meat-analogue pathogen tables; on the 168 endpoints with a complete n=6 replicate source, 164 (97.6%) agreed within 0.01 log10 units. Overall, AgentCount demonstrates the promising potential of agentic workflows for applications in applied microbiology.

Learning Object-Centric Motion Priors from Human for Robotic Dexterous Manipulation

Learning Object-Centric Motion Priors from Human for Robotic Dexterous Manipulation

Zhengdong Hong、Guofeng Zhang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Manipulating diverse objects with multi-fingered dexterous hands is challenging due to the high dimensionality and complex dynamics. Human-Object Interaction (HOI) datasets provide rich knowledge about task information and embodied interactions. Instead of solely imitating the human demonstrations, our method learns to holistically predict future hand-object states by leveraging these datasets. The predicted future states of the object can serve as a general-purpose reward term for reinforcement learning, reducing reliance on task-specific reward engineering and enhancing generalization across tasks. We conduct extensive experiments on three manipulation tasks in simulation and the real world. Our approach outperforms existing SOTA methods in both success rate and generalizability on novel objects. Furthermore, we validate the cross-embodiment compatibility of our methods by successfully deploying the skills on different robot hands.

3D Pose Estimation in Projection Space via Pixel-level Visible Light Communication

3D Pose Estimation in Projection Space via Pixel-level Visible Light Communication

Michikuni Eguchi、Takefumi Hiraki

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Projector-based augmented reality enables natural interaction between users and devices by overlaying digital information onto the physical world through projected imagery. A key technical requirement of such systems is accurate positional alignment between the projector and the devices. Pixel-level Visible Light Communication (PVLC) achieves this alignment without external sensing infrastructure by embedding invisible spatial codes into each projected pixel through high-speed brightness modulation that preserves image quality. However, the information that PVLC transmits is inherently planar. PVLC has therefore been limited to devices moving on a flat surface and cannot estimate the 3D pose of devices in three-dimensional space, such as aerial robots. To extend PVLC-based pose estimation to three dimensions, we formulate it as a Perspective-n-Point (PnP) problem. The 2D pixel positions that four coplanar photosensors on the device receive via PVLC serve as the image points, and their known 3D layout provides the object points. Experiments show that the proposed system estimates 3D pose with a latency of approximately 8~ms and a 3D position RMSE of 19.2~mm after Kalman filtering. These results confirm the first realization of real-time 3D pose estimation coordinated with high-quality projected image content without external sensing infrastructure.

Riemannian Optimization-based Whole-body Obstacle Avoidance for Mobile Continuum Robots in Cluttered Environments

Riemannian Optimization-based Whole-body Obstacle Avoidance for Mobile Continuum Robots in Cluttered Environments

Duo Tang、Ping Deng、Sophie Armanini、Yajing Shen、Toshio Fukuda 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Inspired by the coordination of global motion and local shape adaptation in snakes, elephant trunks, and octopus arms, we propose a unified planning and control framework for mobile continuum robots in cluttered and confined environments. Existing approaches often address high-level path planning and local motion control separately, limiting coordinated base-arm motion, kinematic consistency, and collision avoidance. Our framework integrates long-horizon trajectory planning with real-time geometric refinement under kinematic and environmental constraints. The global layer combines A* search with minimum-jerk planning to generate smooth reference trajectories. The local layer uses a geometry-consistent inverse kinematics solver and Riemannian optimization to coordinate mobile-base motion and continuum deformation while avoiding obstacles. We evaluate the framework through simulated pipe-traversal tasks with multiple baseline planners and real-world experiments involving navigation among dense obstacles and manipulation in confined spaces. Results demonstrate improved motion consistency, feasibility, and adaptability, supporting coordinated whole-body motion of mobile continuum robots in constrained environments.

Beyond Terminal Constraints: Reliable Robot Control with Learned Safe Sets

Beyond Terminal Constraints: Reliable Robot Control with Learned Safe Sets

Gianni Lunardi、Elias Fontanari、Matteo Saveriano、Andrea Del Prete

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Ensuring safety in advanced robot control methods such as Model Predictive Control (MPC) and Reinforcement Learning remains an open challenge. A common approach relies on control-invariant sets, yet their exact computation for nonlinear systems is generally intractable, and numerical approximations compromise theoretical guarantees. In this work, we show that safety does not need to rely exclusively on control invariance. We introduce novel classes of safe sets, each broader than classical control-invariant sets, and analyze corresponding MPC schemes that guarantee recursive feasibility by constraining different nodes of the prediction horizon rather than only the terminal state. Furthermore, when the safe set is an inner approximation of a suitable control-invariant set, our methods can ensure safety through a safe-abort strategy, even in the absence of recursive feasibility. We validate our approaches in extensive simulations on a 6-joint robot manipulator, showing fewer constraint violations than state-of-the-art methods while maintaining comparable tracking performance and computational efficiency. Hardware experiments further demonstrate the practicality of our schemes for real-world robotic applications.

Can Astra Solve RoboMME without Breaking the Bank?

Can Astra Solve RoboMME without Breaking the Bank?

Bingao Chen、Haoquan Fang、C. Karen Liu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robotic manipulation often requires remembering information that is no longer visible, such as an object's location before occlusion or the order of a demonstrated action sequence. A large vision-language planner can read this history, but calling it before every action chunk is slow and costly. We present a three-tier system for the RoboMME benchmark. GPT-6 Astra (System 2) selects a grounded subtask from stored visual history, a $π_{0.5}$ vision-language-action policy (System 1) executes it, and a fine-tuned Qwen3-VL-4B monitor (System 1.5) checks after each action chunk whether the subtask is finished. The instruction is kept until the monitor reports completion or a task-specific event occurs, while the policy keeps acting on new observations. On RoboMME, the system succeeds in 79.13% of episodes with an average of 3.63 Astra calls and 80.84 seconds per episode. This is 34.62 points above the best published non-oracle baseline and 4.95 points below GroundSG+Oracle, which gives the low-level policy ground-truth subgoals.

Robocasa-Mem: Benchmarking Environment and Process Memory in Long-Horizon Mobile Manipulation

Robocasa-Mem: Benchmarking Environment and Process Memory in Long-Horizon Mobile Manipulation

Sihang Chen、Chen Ye、Wei Chen、Xiaohan Lei、Zhipeng Tang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In the real world, mobile manipulation robots must perform complex object interactions and make multi-step decisions, often relying on task-relevant information that is no longer observable. However, existing benchmarks provide limited evaluation of such memory-dependent decision-making in mobile manipulation. To address this gap, we introduce RoboCasa-Mem, a new benchmark comprising nine mobile manipulation tasks that require agents to use interaction history at key decision points. Specifically, the tasks are grouped into two categories of memory: One is environment memory, which captures spatial locations, object attributes, and object relationships. The other is process memory, which captures single-task progress and multi-task scheduling. Particularly, we introduce Behavior-Conditioned Evaluation. For tasks with multiple valid execution paths, instead of fixing a single target or trajectory, the evaluator derives later task constraints from the robot's earlier actions. This allows us to evaluate whether later behavior is consistent with the robot's own interaction history while reducing shortcuts based on fixed target assignments. We provide 1,800 successful demonstrations for policy training. We also develop a baseline on our benchmark, namely Hierarchical Memory-Augmented Policy(HMAP). Its high-level planner uses structured entity and process memories to generate subtasks. Its low-level VLA controller uses subgoal-conditioned queries to incorporate VGGT-Omega geometry into action generation. Together, RoboCasa-Mem and HMAP baseline support systematic evaluation of memory-dependent behavior and the development of memory-aware mobile manipulation policies.

Does RRT* Work on Uneven Terrain Meshes?

Does RRT* Work on Uneven Terrain Meshes?

Otobong Jerome、Tiago Nascimento

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The optimal rapidly exploring random tree (RRT*) is proven asymptotically optimal on open subsets of Euclidean space with straight-line local planning. We ask whether it works on a triangle-mesh terrain, a two-dimensional surface in R^3. First, the original RRT* cannot run on a mesh: the surface has zero volume, so uniform sampling never hits it, no clearance ball fits in it, and chords across a fold leave it. Second, the surface reformulation we call Mesh RRT*, with its naive edge, the chord costed by its length, solved every run on heightfields with slopes up to 75 degrees but minimises the wrong objective: its cost, the straight-line length of a polyline whose vertices lie on the surface while its segments may leave it by a tolerance tau, is the length of no route on the surface and lies strictly below the geodesic length on any curved mesh. In numeric simulation and after 30,000 iterations the reported cost is on average up to 2.7% below the exact geodesic, a cost no path attains, and the returned route on average 0.1-0.5% longer than it. Rewiring keeps the biased edges, the bias decays only linearly with the step on polyhedral surfaces, and shrinking tau trades bias for connectivity. Third, replacing each chord by a path on the surface above it, built from projected points joined across the mesh edges they straddle and costed by its own length, makes every edge cost the exact length of a feasible path; with no other change the exact length of the returned routes is on average within 0.07-0.16% of the geodesic at every roughness tested, two to 3.4 times closer than with chords on the rougher terrains. The planner is in MeshNav3D 0.2.0 (release pending).

2026-11-04

ZipDrift: Generating Multimodal Short-Horizon MPC Costs with One-Step Drifting Models

ZipDrift: Generating Multimodal Short-Horizon MPC Costs with One-Step Drifting Models

Ke Zhang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Short-horizon model predictive control (MPC) is fast but myopic. ZipMPC compensates by learning a context-dependent correction of the short-horizon cost that imitates a long-horizon expert. We observe that the optimal correction is often multimodal: when a slower opponent can be passed on either side, good corrections of opposite sign coexist, and the expert's choice between them hinges on tiny cost differences. Deterministic regression then averages modes or inherits inconsistent expert tie-breaking. We propose ZipDrift, which trains a one-step drifting generator over MPC cost corrections, tilted toward low closed-loop cost and learned only from cost evaluations of candidate corrections, without differentiating through the optimiser. A few generated candidates are ranked by a learned critic, and the model-based MPC then enforces dynamics and constraints. In a kinematic overtaking benchmark derived from ZipMPC, 53% of contexts are bimodal and the manual short-horizon MPC crashes in 28.5% of test episodes. With an eight-dimensional stagewise correction, ZipDrift is collision-free in all 600 test episodes and matches the best evaluated candidate, whereas an idealised ZipMPC-style regressor crashes in 4.0% (16.0% with inconsistent expert labels) and critic-ranked random candidates with the same budget in 2.0%.

Cognitive and Behavioural Effects of Haptic Feedback for ROV Teleoperation in Hazardous Environments

Cognitive and Behavioural Effects of Haptic Feedback for ROV Teleoperation in Hazardous Environments

Joshua Brown、Ildar Farkhatdinov、Michael Jenkin

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Teleoperating a Remotely Operated Vehicle (ROV) in complex underwater environments presents unique challenges. Dynamic, three dimensional obstacles and unseen water currents pose real risks to expensive robotic hardware in environments in which recovery can be next to impossible. This paper explores the cognitive effects of haptic feedback to improve vehicle safety in such environments. An in-water study is analysed with a ROV exposed to controlled cross-currents, with environmental information presented via a soft and vibrating haptic touchpad. Results demonstrate that while encountering an unseen cross-current significantly increases an operator's physical and temporal demand, the introduction of haptic feedback mitigates these factors and improves self-assessed performance. Interestingly, the haptic condition also produced the highest levels of mental demand and operator frustration. Despite this increased mental workload, operators drove the ROV significantly faster when receiving haptic cues compared to the current-only condition, indicating a higher level of user confidence. Ultimately, these findings suggest that while haptic feedback may initially require more cognitive processing to interpret unseen hazards, it successfully enhances operator awareness and promotes more confident navigation in hazardous environments.

OUCH: Objective, Uniform, Controlled Haptic Stimulation for Automated Neurological Assessment

OUCH: Objective, Uniform, Controlled Haptic Stimulation for Automated Neurological Assessment

Sherry Choi、Sitao Sunny Li、Joseph Romero、Lillian Chin、Ashwin G. Ramayya 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Motor responses evoked by noxious stimulation are used to assess neurological function in hospitalized patients with disorders of consciousness. In standard bedside assessments, these stimuli are applied manually, resulting in substantial variation in force, duration, frequency, and location. Here, we present OUCH (Objective, Uniform, Controlled Haptic stimulation), a wrist-worn device that uses pneumatic actuation to deliver noxious mechanical stimuli. OUCH combines stacked pneumatic pouch actuators, a rigid indenter, and a compliant spacer designed to buckle at a set load, so that the indenter contacts the skin only during actuation. We characterize the device using blocked force tests, clinician evaluation, finite element analysis, and a deformable pressure testbed, and benchmark it against a commercially available handheld pressure algometer. At 200 kPa pouch inflation pressure, our indenter concentrated its load into an estimated contact pressure of 18,500 kPa, over 25 times that of the algometer tip, while requiring less total force. On the deformable testbed, it localized deformation near the surface, while deformation at depth remained comparable to the algometer. As a result, in preliminary perceptual testing, our indenter elicited discomfort at approximately 89% lower applied force than the algometer, demonstrating the feasibility of evoking discomfort with a compact, wearable soft actuator. These results show that combining pneumatic actuation with designed contact geometry enables controlled, repeatable noxious stimulation at the wrist, providing a foundation for objective neurological assessment.

2026-11-04

Dynamic Shape Control of Soft Trunk Robots

Dynamic Shape Control of Soft Trunk Robots

Hehui Zheng、Marc Stampanoni、Roshan S. Kaundinya、Ronan Hinchet、George Haller 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Controlling the shape profile of soft trunk robots extends their reach into complex regions of the workspace and enhances their use in practical applications. Recent advances in soft robot design have enabled soft trunk robots to achieve greater flexibility in conforming to complex shapes. Specifically, trunk robots whose geometries are designed as helicoids have been shown to enable extreme shape-changing capabilities. Their compliance makes them highly nonlinear, which in turn makes shape control difficult. On similar tendon-driven trunk robots, model-based controllers based on the theory of adiabatic spectral submanifolds (aSSMs) have achieved high accuracy in closed-loop end-effector tracking. We extend this approach by learning an aSSM model of the full body of a trimmed-helicoid soft trunk robot from data and use this in a model predictive control (MPC) scheme to demonstrate a range of shape control tasks on hardware. On closed-loop dynamic shape regulation, our method outperforms the available linear baselines by a factor of 10. In human-in-the-loop interactive demos, our method completes section-by-section shaping and balances a tennis ball for over 25 seconds, while the linear baselines fall short. A model-free reinforcement learning policy trained for 12 hours on hardware fails on the same balancing task. These results establish aSSM-based MPC as a practical route to full-body shape control on real soft hardware.

FOSCo-Net: Ego-centric Voxel Completion for Forest LiDAR Occlusions

FOSCo-Net: Ego-centric Voxel Completion for Forest LiDAR Occlusions

Iman Esfandiyar、Dominik Belter

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Ground robots operating in temperate forests suffer from systematic LiDAR occlusions caused by tree trunks, creating persistent blind regions that degrade traversability estimation. In this paper, we propose FOSCo-Net, a self-supervised voxel completion framework that learns to reconstruct occluded geometry from a single scan using temporally accumulated observations as automatic supervision, and we evaluate cylindrical and Cartesian ego-centric grids with multiple decoder designs. FOSCo-Net consistently improves reconstruction quality and traversal safety, with a gated skip decoder achieving the most reliable performance and significantly reducing false obstacle predictions compared to baselines.

$A^3$-VLA: Automatic Perception-Guided Attention Alignment for Robot Manipulation

$A^3$-VLA: Automatic Perception-Guided Attention Alignment for Robot Manipulation

Shengzhe Zhang、Qi Zhang、Dazhong Shen、Chao Wang、Shaopeng Zhai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-Language-Action Models (VLAs) enable robots to perform manipulation tasks by leveraging Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to process visual observations and language instructions. However, the action prediction objective itself lacks fine-grained task-specific perceptual supervision, making it prone to relying on task-irrelevant visual shortcuts, leading attention away from target objects and key interaction areas, thereby limiting its operational performance and generalization ability. To address this, we propose Automatic Perception-Guided Attention Alignment for Robot Manipulation ($A^3$-VLA), a framework that derives task-specific visual guidance from the manipulation process itself. Specifically, $A^3$-VLA first generates heuristic candidate regions for target objects and the robot from interaction-induced visual changes, and refines them with a visual segmentation model offline. These regions serve as explicit perceptual guidance through two complementary objectives: an attention allocation loss that encourages sufficient focus on task-critical regions when language or robot state tokens query the visual input, and a contrastive learning loss that aligns the representations of candidate target regions with language instructions while separating them from task-irrelevant regions. Extensive experiments on simulated and real-world manipulation tasks demonstrate that $A^3$-VLA consistently improves multiple VLA backbones, with an average success rate gain of 6.5% - 25.2% across benchmarks.

TwinBridge: Digital Twin-Driven Policy and Reward Transfer for Real-World Reinforcement Learning in Laboratory Robotics

TwinBridge: Digital Twin-Driven Policy and Reward Transfer for Real-World Reinforcement Learning in Laboratory Robotics

Shu'ang Sun、Yibo Qiu、Haoliang Ye、Zan Huang、Ao Tian 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Online reinforcement learning offers an effective approach to precise laboratory manipulation, yet costly real-world demonstrations and limited access to dense process rewards hinder its practical application. We present TwinBridge, a digital twin-driven framework that supplies policy initialization and dense visual rewards for real-world online learning. We construct digital twins by combining 3D Gaussian splatting with physics simulation. Here, simulator states provide task events and geometric progress under physical prerequisites and ordering constraints, yielding rewards for policy pretraining with simulated demonstrations and reinforcement learning. We distill this supervision into a visual reward model. During real-world interaction, its predictions are combined with execution history to compute dense rewards for human-in-the-loop policy refinement. Across five real-world laboratory tasks, TwinBridge achieves a final mean autonomous success rate of 98% with at most 25 minutes of real-world interaction per task. Experiments show that dense rewards improve interaction efficiency over sparse rewards, and simulation-based policy initialization further accelerates learning.

Scale Anchoring via Implicit Semantics and Sequence-Consistent Loop Closure for VGGT-Based SLAM

Scale Anchoring via Implicit Semantics and Sequence-Consistent Loop Closure for VGGT-Based SLAM

Ziyi Jin、Kangjin Yu、Beichen Wang、Can Jiang、Zhongpu Xia 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Feed-forward geometric foundation models enable dense monocular reconstruction without requiring camera calibration, but independently reconstructed submaps can accumulate scale drift, and even a valid revisit can yield an inconsistent cross-submap measurement. We extend VGGT-SLAM 2.0 with fixed-root scale anchoring and short-sequence-assisted direct loop connections. SALAD selects an image pair between the initial submap and each eligible new submap; joint VGGT inference then uses learned implicit semantic-geometric cues to supply a scale-only anchor while preserving the original overlap chain. For loop closure, neighboring image pairs support a consistency check and translation-scale alignment. Each accepted candidate contributes one robust Sim(3) factor between existing center-frame nodes, without an auxiliary loop submap. A 17-sequence comparison lowers mean Sim(3) ATE on nine TUM sequences from 0.1018 to 0.0451 m relative to the VGGT-SLAM 2.0 baseline. Anchoring improves all seven no-loop module ablations. On three Clio scenes, a fixed-loop comparison favors the direct connection pipeline over the tested auxiliary-submap pipeline. A separate candidate audit exposes additional rejections of visually overlapping pairs by the short-sequence check. These comparisons distinguish the gains from long-range scale anchoring and loop connection choices from the limitations of candidate verification.

CoSafe: Cooperative Runtime Safety Assurance for Mixed-Traffic Autonomous Driving

CoSafe: Cooperative Runtime Safety Assurance for Mixed-Traffic Autonomous Driving

Dingji Wang、You Lu、Haowen Jiang、Yanggang Chen、Yuyang Bi 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

With the rapid development of autonomous driving systems (ADSes), real-world roads are evolving into mixed-traffic environments where autonomous vehicles (AVs) and other traffic participants (e.g., human-driven vehicles (HDVs), cyclists, and pedestrians) coexist and interact with each other. However, current ADSes still incur various safety violations in mixed traffic scenarios, raising a strong demand for runtime safety assurance frameworks that can continuously monitor hazards and prevent potential violations. Existing ego-centric assurance approaches are insufficient for mixed traffic, where violations often arise from locally reasonable yet jointly incompatible decisions among multiple vehicles and cannot be reliably resolved through independent takeover. To bridge this gap, we propose CoSafe, a modular and ADS-agnostic cooperative runtime safety assurance framework for mixed-traffic autonomous driving to extend runtime safety assurance from ego-centric takeover to scene-level cooperative intervention. CoSafe leverages vehicle-to-everything (V2X) communication to enable roadside units (RSUs) to monitor scene-level hazards and jointly mitigate hazards involving multiple vehicles. We integrate CoSafe with seven state-of-the-art ADSes, and our evaluation on the InterDrive benchmark demonstrates that CoSafe improves the driving score of AVs by 70.03% on average, reduces safety violations by 87.80%, and accurately identifies unsafe AVs with an F3 score of 0.930, while satisfying real-time constraints.

GPUMAP: GPU-Accelerated Progressive Uniform Manifold Approximation and Projection

GPUMAP: GPU-Accelerated Progressive Uniform Manifold Approximation and Projection

Myeongwon Jung、Jaemin Jo

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Dimensionality reduction (DR) techniques are fundamental to the analysis of high-dimensional data, yet their substantial computational cost limits their use in interactive settings. To address this limitation, we present GPU-accelerated Progressive Uniform Manifold Approximation and Projection (GPUMAP), an efficient progressive DR algorithm designed for interactive use. We identify three atomic operations within UMAP and restructure them into a progressive pipeline. As part of this adaptation, we introduce modifications to UMAP, including operation queues, instance-wise local join, and scheduling strategies. We further accelerate computation by leveraging GPU parallelism, enabling interactive analysis of million-scale datasets on commodity GPU hardware. Finally, we employ our implementation to systematically study the behavior of progressive DR, investigating how projections evolve over time relative to the ground-truth projection and how biased data streams influence the quality and stability of the resulting projections.

The Effectiveness of Sliding Mode Control for Robust Vision Stabilization under the Plant-Binding Condition

The Effectiveness of Sliding Mode Control for Robust Vision Stabilization under the Plant-Binding Condition

Sooyeun Yang、Jongseong Brad Choi、Seung Bok choi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Boundary-layer Sliding Mode Control (SMC) has been applied to vision-based stabilization, but identifying its activation condition against simpler causal baselines (LPF, PID) has been elusive. The operational condition is plant binding: the disturbance velocity approaches or exceeds the actuator slew limit, so the bounded per-step actuation budget binds. We validate this in two independent vision domains: handheld translation stabilization (12 1080p clips; LPF, PID, SMC, L1) and angular re-framing against a virtual slew-rate-limited pan-tilt-zoom (PTZ) plant. Outside binding, leave-one-out cross-validation makes SMC and LPF on translation statistically indistinguishable, and PID beats SMC on the angular 99th-percentile pointing offset by 5.85 degrees (95% CI clear of zero); inside binding -- reached only by sweeping the simulated slew limit, as both captured datasets land outside it -- the translation gap stays a tie and SMC beats PID on the angular worst case (Cohen's d up to +0.83). The PTZ plant's 100 degrees/s default sits on the cross-over, which explains a small-margin PID result previously reported in the angular setting. A complementary ablation shows raising the SMC reaching gain k costs mean tracking outside a narrow window.

2026-11-03

MESA: An Evaluation Framework for Compositional, Semantic, and Spatial Generalization in Robotics

MESA: An Evaluation Framework for Compositional, Semantic, and Spatial Generalization in Robotics

Albert Wilcox、Frank Chang、Nhi Nguyen、Aishani Chakraborty、Jeremy A. Collins 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Despite growing interest in foundation models for robotic manipulation, systematic evaluation of their capabilities remains elusive. Existing benchmarks are either narrow and saturated, offering little signal on SOTA methods, broadly scoped, providing diversity at the cost of controlled experimentation, or require training on large, messy open-source datasets. We introduce MESA, an evaluation framework designed for precise, reproducible measurement of language-conditioned policy generalization. MESA provides five evaluation suites that isolate in-distribution performance and four distinct axes of generalization: spatial configuration, object instance, object category, and subtask composition, enabling researchers to diagnose where and why policies fail, not just whether they fail. To populate these suites at scale, we develop MESA-Gen, a pipeline for task and demonstration generation built by improving MimicGen. We instantiate the framework on two platforms and release both: the single-arm MESA suite, and BiMESA, the first large-scale benchmark for bimanual generalization. We evaluate seven baseline methods spanning diffusion policies, VLM-based approaches, and pretrained VLAs, finding that the strongest model achieves only 52% on MESA and 49% on BiMESA, providing meaningful signal while leaving ample room for progress. MESA rankings are broadly consistent with RoboArena scores, suggesting that MESA captures method-level differences relevant beyond simulation. Project website: https://pairlab.github.io/MESA.

Despite growing interest in foundation models for robotic manipulation, systematic evaluation of their capabilities remains elusive. Existing benchmarks are either narrow and saturated, offering little signal on SOTA methods, broadly scoped, providing diversity at the cost of controlled experimentation, or require training on large, messy open-source datasets. We introduce MESA, an evaluation framework designed for precise, reproducible measurement of language-conditioned policy generalization. Deep-Learning Algorithm for Indeterminate Thyroid Nodules: Comparison and Combination with Molecular Testing

Deep-Learning Algorithm for Indeterminate Thyroid Nodules: Comparison and Combination with Molecular Testing

Jichen Yang、Jeffrey Ashton、Jikai Zhang、Maciej A. Mazurowski、Benjamin Wildman-Tobriner 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Thyroid nodules with indeterminate cytology (Bethesda III) carry an intermediate risk of malignancy, and molecular testing is widely used to decide between surgery and surveillance, but it is costly and not universally available. We investigated whether a convolutional neural network (CNN) applied to routine ultrasound images can stratify Bethesda III nodules, and how it compares and combines with molecular testing. We retrospectively identified 466 nodule records from 418 patients who underwent molecular testing (Afirma or ThyroSeq). A CNN pre-trained on determinate nodules was adapted to indeterminate nodules with soft labels and transfer learning. After removal of duplicate records, 119 nodules with surgical pathology were used for development and 47 were held out for testing. On the test set, the deep learning (DL) model achieved an AUC of 0.73 (95% CI 0.51-0.94), compared with 0.55 (0.38-0.71) for molecular testing (P = 0.15). At a cut-off inherited from the original algorithm, the DL model was more specific than molecular testing (0.95 vs 0.32; P < 0.001), with a higher positive predictive value (0.67 vs 0.21), a lower sensitivity (0.44 vs 0.78; P = 0.25) and a similar negative predictive value (0.88 vs 0.86). All six DL-positive test nodules were molecular-positive, and the DL output split the molecular-positive nodules into a high-risk (4/6 malignant) and a lower-risk group (3/27 malignant; P = 0.01). Surgical verification was strongly driven by the molecular result (99% of positive vs 19% of negative results), and molecular-test accuracy depended on the platform. A logistic regression of the DL output, nodule size and the molecular result dropped the DL term and did not improve on the DL output alone. Ultrasound DL complemented molecular testing for Bethesda III nodules, in particular by identifying molecular-positive nodules at lower risk of malignancy.

LumiTac: Compact, High-Rate Six-Axis Optical Tactile Sensing for Human-Robot Interaction

LumiTac: Compact, High-Rate Six-Axis Optical Tactile Sensing for Human-Robot Interaction

Hao Wu、Haotian Guo、Yu Feng、Yutong Wang、Jianshu Zhou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Integrating six-axis tactile feedback into robotic interfaces requires compact sensors with accurate load measurement and rapid online reconstruction. This paper presents LumiTac, a low-profile, camera-free optoelectronic tactile sensor combining spatially distributed illumination with spectral encoding. Five photoemitters and five RGBW photoreceivers are mounted on opposing circuit boards coupled through translucent silicone. Load-induced interlayer motion modulates the internal light field under compression, shear, bending, and torsion. The sensing head measures 12 mm in diameter and 4.75 mm in thickness, weighs less than 2 g, and has a component cost of approximately USD 7. A compact multilayer perceptron reconstructs three force and three moment components from 20 optical channels. Independent testing yielded coefficients of determination (R-squared) of 0.9657-0.9941, with mean absolute errors of 0.054-0.190 N for forces and 0.304-1.419 mN m for moments. Online prediction achieved an average update rate of 303.57 Hz and a median software processing latency of 0.722 ms. Experiments involving combined loading, light weights, and rapid tapping evaluated load tracking, sensitivity to small forces, and transient response, respectively. Robotic demonstrations further validated its applicability to tactile-guided manipulation and physical human-robot interaction. This study paves the way for compact, high-rate optical tactile sensing in robotic manipulation and human-robot interaction.

RACE: Calibrated Cumulative Scoring for Rollout-Free Robot Policy Checkpoint Selection

RACE: Calibrated Cumulative Scoring for Rollout-Free Robot Policy Checkpoint Selection

Changyeon Kim、Yekyung Nah、Kimin Lee

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Assessing the deployment performance of learned robot policies during training is challenging, as directly measuring task success requires costly real-world trials for each candidate checkpoint. Averaged validation loss offers a rollout-free alternative, but its checkpoint rankings can be inconsistent with deployment performance. We analyze two limitations of this criterion: it penalizes action deviations without considering whether the policy can recover from them, and gives later reference predictions equal weight even when earlier deviations may lead execution away from the reference. We propose RACE, a novel rollout-free metric for ranking checkpoints using a fixed set of held-out successful trajectories. RACE calibrates action deviations against state-conditional thresholds derived from action differences at similar states in these trajectories. We aggregate these scores through a running product, so that a substantial deviation reduces the contribution of subsequent reference predictions. Averaging the resulting cumulative scores over time and across trajectories yields the checkpoint score. In simulation, RACE outperforms baselines across validation sources, while several baselines degrade sharply when the validation set changes. On real robots, its rankings align with deployment success across the evaluated policy backbones and tasks of varying difficulty. Perturbation analyses further show that RACE distinguishes deviations with markedly different effects on task success, even when baselines respond similarly.

H^2MARL: Hierarchical Heterogeneous Multi-agent Reinforcement Learning for Humanoid Robot Control

H^2MARL: Hierarchical Heterogeneous Multi-agent Reinforcement Learning for Humanoid Robot Control

Xuemin Hu、Kaixin Li、Qingqing Xia、Yuxin Wang、Wei Xie 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Coordinated whole-body control of humanoid robots remains challenging due to high-dimensional coupled dynamics and substantial physical heterogeneity across joint degrees of freedom (JDoFs). Existing multi-agent reinforcement learning methods either employ coarse agent partitions or directly apply generic heterogeneous-agent algorithms, while hierarchical controllers commonly optimize high-level planning and low-level execution independently, leading to uncoordinated motion. In this paper, we propose H^2MARL, a hierarchical heterogeneous multi-agent reinforcement learning framework with two key mechanisms. We first formulate humanoid whole-body control using a JDoF-level physical-heterogeneity-aware policy factorization, where each torque-actuated JDoF is conditioned on an explicit descriptor of its actuation, kinematic, and dynamic properties. Moreover, we develop a closed-loop policy-executor co-optimization mechanism that jointly adapts the high-level pose planner and the low-level joint executor using shared interaction trajectories, thereby reducing the distribution mismatch between planning and execution. Extensive experiments in MuJoCo and Isaac Gym demonstrate that H^2MARL consistently improves locomotion stability, gait coordination, and motion quality over homogeneous, coarse-grained, and independently optimized alternatives.

VABAR: A Variational Automation Benchmark for Agentic Robotics

VABAR: A Variational Automation Benchmark for Agentic Robotics

Shuangyu Xie、Kaiyuan Chen、Uksang Yoo、Xinyue Zhu、Meadow Shen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

For robots to operate reliably in commercial and industrial applications, they must persistently perform specific tasks with known variations in object shape and pose. We call this Variational Automation (VA) to differentiate these settings from fixed automation. Inspired by recent papers on Agentic Robotics, where pretrained LLM and VLM coding agents combine interpretable robot programming with the open-world adaptability of model free policies, we propose a general set of agentic robotics benchmark design criteria that includes accessibility, repeatability, diversity, relevance to agentic robotics and practical applications, physical realizability, resistance to benchmark saturation, and computational efficiency. We instantiate these principles in six manipulation task classes spanning industrial, medical, and infrastructure applications: tool sorting, tool hanging, gear meshing, syringe packing, cable insertion, and cable routing. We introduce the Variational Automation Benchmark for Agentic Robotics (VABAR), an open, easily replicable, simulation-based manipulation benchmark to systematically evaluating agentic robotics systems. Built fully on open-source tools, VABAR features detailed implementations using the Newton physics engine, standardized task variations, difficulty tiers, success metrics, and a library of 33 generalizable skills with human-authored programmatic reference solutions. We provide initial baseline evaluations using an open agentic manipulation harness, Graph-as-Policy (GaP), and Isaac Lab Arena.

遥感
2026-11-04

ConDR: Conservative Reachability through Neural Hamilton-Jacobi Subsolutions

ConDR: Conservative Reachability through Neural Hamilton-Jacobi Subsolutions

Kendra Givens、Taylor T. Johnson、Bryan C. Ward、Thomas Beckers

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Hamilton-Jacobi (HJ) Reachability identifies the unsafe states of a dynamical system from which it can be forced into failure under worst-case disturbance. However, classical grid-based solvers scale exponentially with state dimension. DeepReach improves scalability by replacing the grid with a neural value function trained on the HJ-Isaacs (HJI) variational inequality (VI), but penalizes both sides of the residual equally. This allows the learned value to lie above the true one and unsafe states to be misclassified as safe. We propose Conservative DeepReach (ConDR) which aims to learn a subsolution of the HJI VI such that the learned unsafe set contains the true one. ConDR enforces the obstacle branch and terminal condition by construction, pushes the HJI residual to a safety margin $ε$ below zero with a one-sided loss, and prevents the degenerate solution of classifying everything as unsafe. We prove that the exact solution of the margin-shifted VI that ConDR trains toward is a subsolution of the original VI so its unsafe set contains the true one and its value lies within $ετ$ of the true value function. We compare against DeepReach and MADR in the established systems of Air3D, Air6D, a 9D multi-vehicle system, and a 13D quadrotor. ConDR reports the lowest rate of unsafe state misclassified and the lowest closed-loop collision rates with at most a small increase in intervention. On Air3D and Air6D, ConDR tolerates model mismatch that scales with $ε$. At matched false-negative rates, ConDR's unsafe sets are smaller than the baselines showing the safety is not obtained by trivial over-approximation.

$\mathcal{C}^\infty$ Smooth Trajectory Planning with Application to Soft Robots

$\mathcal{C}^\infty$ Smooth Trajectory Planning with Application to Soft Robots

Reinhard M. Grassmann、Abdulaziz Y. Alkayas、Lingyun Chen、Sami Haddadin

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

High motion performance and avoiding structural vibrations rely on smooth trajectory planning. The smoothness requirement depends on the robot's complexity in terms of the number of degrees of freedom and system order due to the flexibility of the joints and links. For industrial robots, the smoothness requirements have been determined and subsequently taken into account. Although unknown for soft continuum robots, the exact smoothness requirement can potentially be much larger than previously considered ones such as $\mathbf{\mathcal{C}^4}$ for collaborative robots and $\mathbf{\mathcal{C}^5}$ for quadcopter drones. To address this gap, we introduce a $\mathbf{\mathcal{C}^\infty}$-smooth trajectory generator that respects defined kinematic limits and generates infinitely differentiable trajectories. The generated path addresses and generalizes to all smoothness requirements, where a transition between sequential states is needed. Therefore, all trajectories have continuous velocity, acceleration, jerk, snap, crackle, pop, etc. Of particular interest is the case of null boundary conditions, i.e., all derivatives at the start and end of the trajectory are equal to zero. We verified and evaluated our proposed approach in simulations and on hardware.
