2026-11-03

Ctrl-F: Force as an Interface for Humanoid Loco-Manipulation

Ctrl-F: Force as an Interface for Humanoid Loco-Manipulation

Mengjie Zhao、Yuxin Chen、Yiyang Shao、Chuye Hong、Jiakang Chen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Humanoid motion tracking has scaled to general policies that reproduce diverse human motion, but the resulting behavior is in free-space: the tracker knows where the body should be, not how it should interact with the environment. Other work makes the object part of the objective, yet abstracts that interaction to contact or geometry, which leaves the demanding cases under-explored. We argue that force is a natural command channel between motion imitation and object interaction, one that specifies not just where to make contact but how hard. We present Ctrl-F, a hierarchical motion-force framework that augments motion tracking with an explicit force command, a 3-D force at each designated contact body such as a hand or a foot. The hierarchical framework comprises: (1) A unified motion-force controller tracks the reference motion while applying the commanded force to the world. (2) A high-level force planner that issues a force command online based on object state and proprioception observations. In simulation and on hardware, Ctrl-F completes loco-manipulation that demands large, sustained interaction force and adjusts its command online as the load changes, both out of reach of a force-blind tracker. On hardware, Ctrl-F demonstrates hand-based dragging, pushing, and lifting, as well as foot-based kicking, successfully manipulating boxes weighing up to 20kg in pushing and kicking tasks where the force-blind baseline fails, while adapting online to additional loads introduced mid-task.

Neuromorphic Camera for Multi-Target Pointing Acquisition and Tracking in Free Space Optical Communication

Neuromorphic Camera for Multi-Target Pointing Acquisition and Tracking in Free Space Optical Communication

Francesco Nardo、Nigitha Selvaraj、Klaus Schertler、Peter Krötz、Ricardo Barrios 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pointing, Acquisition, and Tracking (PAT) in Free-Space Optical Communication (FSOC) applications must achieve both low jitter and a large Field of View (FoV) to support beacon acquisition and multi-target tracking. While conventional frame-based sensors provide the necessary angular coverage, their performance is limited by latency, available frame-rate, and motion blur. This work explores and demonstrates the application of an event-based vision sensor for PAT tasks in FSOC. The system employs a neuromorphic sensor that asynchronously records brightness changes (events) with microsecond-level temporal resolution. The main contribution of this paper is a comparative analysis of event-based tracking algorithms for the simultaneous acquisition and tracking of multiple optical beacons that operate directly on this asynchronous stream. The performance of these strategies is validated on an experimental dataset representing realistic operational conditions for dynamic High-Altitude Platform (HAP) and Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) networks. The analysis compares key metrics, such as tracking accuracy and processing latency, under various signal-to-background conditions.

Representations of 3D Rotations: Mathematical Foundations and Comparative Analysis

Representations of 3D Rotations: Mathematical Foundations and Comparative Analysis

Aizierjiang Aiersilan、Haochen Liu、James Hahn

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Rotation representations are foundational in fields such as computer graphics, robotics, and machine learning, where precise and efficient modeling of 3D orientations is critical. This paper comprehensively investigates diverse representations of the special orthogonal group $SO(3)$, such as Euler angles, axis-angle vectors, quaternions, rotation matrices, exponential maps, and emerging continuous and probabilistic methods, evaluating their mathematical formulations, continuity, susceptibility to gimbal lock, computational efficiency, storage requirements, interpolation properties, and composition operations, while integrating detailed algebraic insights with practical applications in fields like animation, pose estimation, inertial navigation, 3D shape registration, and neural networks. Empirical evidence highlights quaternions' dominance due to their compactness and computational efficiency, while alternatives like 6D continuous representations and matrix Fisher distributions provide enhanced continuity and uncertainty modeling. Future research could explore hybrid methods and thorough large-scale evaluations to help build a solid foundation for improving rotation representation techniques.

Online Adaptation to Latent Physical Properties for Learning from Demonstration

Online Adaptation to Latent Physical Properties for Learning from Demonstration

Kotaro Kajitsuka、Masaki Murooka、Fumio Kanehiro

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Manipulating visually identical objects with different physical properties requires adapting actions based on information acquired through interaction. We address the problem of learning from demonstrations how to adapt manipulation actions, such as regrasping and changing contact locations, based on physical-property information acquired during interaction. To this end, we propose a learning-from-demonstration method that learns latent representations of physical properties needed to predict object responses and conditions a policy on online estimates of these representations. A dynamics model and object-specific latent representations are jointly learned from demonstrations that include behaviors adapting to the objects' physical properties, and the learned representations are then used to condition an imitation policy. During execution, the dynamics model and policy weights remain fixed, while the latent representation is updated based on the discrepancy between predicted and actual observations, allowing interaction with the object to inform action generation. Real-world and simulation experiments with objects whose centers of mass differ suggest that online adaptation through latent representations of physical properties can improve task success rates. However, in simulation, success rates on center-of-mass locations absent from the demonstrations remained around 30%, indicating limited adaptation to unseen physical properties. Project page: https://isri-aist.github.io/latent-dynamics-project-page/

To this end, we propose a learning-from-demonstration method that learns latent representations of physical properties needed to predict object responses and conditions a policy on online estimates of these representations. A dynamics model and object-specific latent representations are jointly learned from demonstrations that include behaviors adapting to the objects' physical properties, and the learned representations are then used to condition an imitation policy. During execution, the dynamics model and policy weights remain fixed, while the latent representation is updated based on the discrepancy between predicted and actual observations, allowing interaction with the object to inform action generation. Real-world and simulation experiments with objects whose centers of mass differ suggest that online adaptation through latent representations of physical properties can improve task success rates. However, in simulation, success rates on center-of-mass locations absent from the demonstrations remained around 30%, indicating limited adaptation to unseen physical properties. Project page: https://isri-aist.github.io/latent-dynamics-project-page/

目标检测/分割生成模型/扩散机器人视觉
2026-11-04

Closed-Loop Adaptive Sampling on a Generative Ocean Model

Closed-Loop Adaptive Sampling on a Generative Ocean Model

Shayesteh Hafezi、Alan Papalia

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Adaptive sampling can substantially improve the value of sparse ocean observations by directing mobile robots toward measurements that are most informative for an evolving environment. Recent learned generative ocean models can produce high-resolution forecasts and ensembles at substantially lower computational cost than traditional numerical models, creating an opportunity to use rich predictive forecasts directly in closed-loop robotic sampling. However, these models do not natively provide the explicit uncertainty representations assumed by classical informative-planning methods, and their forecasts often evolve in learned latent spaces rather than directly in physical ocean variables. We develop a multi-robot closed-loop adaptive sampling framework that uses a single learned generative model for forecasting, uncertainty estimation, planning, and measurement assimilation. The model forecasts an ensemble in latent space, decodes it to physical ocean states, and uses ensemble statistics to construct A- or D-optimal sampling objectives. We select sampling locations subject to robot reachability under predicted currents. The collected measurements are assimilated back into the latent state using observation-conditioned diffusion sampling. Across 120 test scenarios A- and D-optimal sampling generally outperforms static, random, and systematic-coverage baselines while adding little planning overhead. Our experiments uncover several scenarios in which model uncertainty diverges from forecast error, motivating future work in uncertainty quantification.

MvBody: Multi-View-Based Hybrid Transformer Using Optical 3D Body Scan for Explainable Cesarean Section Prediction

MvBody: Multi-View-Based Hybrid Transformer Using Optical 3D Body Scan for Explainable Cesarean Section Prediction

Ruting Cheng、Boyuan Feng、Yijiang Zheng、Chuhui Qiu、Aizierjiang Aiersilan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Accurately assessing the risk of cesarean section (CS) delivery is critical, especially in settings with limited medical resources, where access to healthcare is often restricted. Early and reliable risk prediction allows better-informed prenatal care decisions and can improve maternal and neonatal outcomes. However, most existing predictive models are tailored for in-hospital use during labor and rely on parameters that are often unavailable in resource-limited or home-based settings. In this study, we conduct a pilot investigation to examine the feasibility of using 3D body shape for CS risk assessment for future applications with more affordable general devices. We propose a novel multi-view-based Transformer network, MvBody, which predicts CS risk using only self-reported medical data and 3D optical body scans obtained between the 31st and 38th weeks of gestation. To enhance training efficiency and model generalizability in data-scarce environments, we incorporate a metric learning loss into the network. Compared to widely used machine learning models and the latest advanced 3D analysis methods, our method demonstrates superior performance, achieving an accuracy of 84.62% and an Area Under the Receiver Operating Characteristic Curve (AUC-ROC) of 0.724 on the independent test set. To improve transparency and trust in the model's predictions, we apply the Integrated Gradients algorithm to provide theoretically grounded explanations of the model's decision-making process. Our results indicate that pre-pregnancy weight, maternal age, obstetric history, previous CS history, and body shape, particularly around the head and shoulders, are key contributors to CS risk prediction.

Vortex-Induced Torque in Grasping Soft Objects: Influence of Geometry and Pneumatic Parameters

Vortex-Induced Torque in Grasping Soft Objects: Influence of Geometry and Pneumatic Parameters

Roman Mykhailyshyn、Tomohiro Motoda、Ivan Virgala、Ann Majewicz Fey、Yukiyasu Domae 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vortex grippers are commonly integrated into automated systems for handling objects with complex geometries in packaging, sorting, and assembling. However, their interaction with compliant objects remains difficult to predict, particularly with respect to the vortex-induced torque transmitted during grasping. This study investigates vortex-induced torque during the grasping of compliant objects and quantifies the coupled effects of supply pressure, tangential nozzle diameter, surface geometry, and curvature. The results show that increasing the tangential nozzle diameter primarily enhances the sensitivity of the generated torque to supply pressure rather than producing a uniformly higher torque. Surface geometry also strongly influences the rotational interaction, with dome-concave surfaces generally producing substantially greater torque than corresponding dome-convex surfaces, whereas the difference between cylindrical concave and convex surfaces is comparatively small. The strongest and most repeatable response was observed for a dome-concave surface exhibiting favorable object-gripper geometric matching. Among the evaluated machine-learning regression methods, Gaussian Process Regression provided the best overall predictive performance under repeated cross-validation, demonstrating that vortex-induced torque can be estimated from combined pneumatic and geometric parameters. These findings provide a basis for predicting and utilizing vortex-induced torque in the dexterous manipulation of soft objects.

Corridor-Based Monitoring and Emergency Braking Decision for Automated Vehicle Safety

Corridor-Based Monitoring and Emergency Braking Decision for Automated Vehicle Safety

Junnan Pan、Vladislav Nenchev、Mohak Mansharamani、Prodromos Sotiriadis、Ferdinand Englberger 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper addresses emergency-braking decisions for a redundant safety channel in automated vehicles. The channel is responsible for identifying hazards from sensor data and determining whether braking is necessary, given the constrained computational resources of embedded hardware. We present an emergency-braking approach that uses a predicted motion corridor to filter \ac{lidar} measurements and then decide whether the ego vehicle must brake. \ac{pmcm} constructs a curvature-aware boundary from the planned path and vehicle geometry so that the monitored region contains the full swept body on both straight and curved paths. \ac{gdeta} then decides whether the ego vehicle must command an emergency brake for objects inside this boundary, scoring geometric occupancy and temporal urgency independently. Hard gates permit braking when either source is conclusive; soft-OR arbitration combines partial evidence when neither gate is active. Consecutive-scan confirmation reduces sensitivity to transient measurement noise. Corridor coverage is evaluated in controlled collision simulations and recorded campus driving. Braking decisions are evaluated in Monte Carlo simulations of lead-vehicle braking and transverse-crossing encounters.

PyRUA-REAL: Robot Exploration and Adaptive Learning through RSI in the Real World

PyRUA-REAL: Robot Exploration and Adaptive Learning through RSI in the Real World

Qiang Wang、Jianxin Bi、Shulong Jiang、Ruiyang Si、Wenbo Huang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Coding agents powered by models such as GPT-6-Astra connect spatial reasoning with executable robot behavior, raising the question of how such agents can produce lasting improvements in robot capabilities from physical interaction. In this work, we study embodied recursive self-improvement (RSI) through real-world exploration, code refinement, and deployment using PyRUA-REAL, a framework in which coding agents retain interaction experience as persistent memory and reusable executable skills that inform subsequent exploration and improvement. Our empirical study places physical manipulation at the center of the improvement process, examining how agents collaborate, what kinds of execution feedback support effective code refinement, and how task specifications and previously acquired skills influence robustness and task transfer. We further investigate the efficiency and capability of the resulting programs when deployed without involving an online agent for decision-making. Across eight real-world tasks, PyRUA-REAL achieves an average success rate of 91.1%. In complementary evaluation on 42 tasks from the RoboDojo benchmark, it achieves an average success rate of 84.4%. This study provides empirical guidance for turning physical exploration into reusable robot skills that support continued improvement and robust, efficient deployment.

Where Is VLA Inference Time Spent? Bottlenecks and Optimization Interactions Across GPUs

Where Is VLA Inference Time Spent? Bottlenecks and Optimization Interactions Across GPUs

Muhammad Danish Waseem、Ahmed Ali-Eldin Hassan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language-action (VLA) models map camera observations and language instructions to robot actions. However, their large backbones and repeated action generation can make inference take longer than the robot's real-time requirements for its next action. To reduce the load of VLA computations, researchers have proposed multiple optimization approaches, including quantization and pruning. These optimizations can be combined with other general optimizations such as better kernel compilation or using CUDA graphs instead of the eager mode. However, most of these optimization results are usually reported on a single GPU type, typically for a few VLA models, with no analysis of what the real bottlenecks are for a model on a particular hardware or how these optimizations would generalize beyond the reported results. In this paper, we provide a comprehensive measurement study of seven families of inference optimization methods, from CUDA graphs and compilation to token pruning and quantization, on seven state-of-the-art VLAs evaluated on four GPU families ranging from a datacenter H100 to an embedded Jetson Thor. We analyse how CPU launch overhead, memory bandwidth, and kernel efficiency jointly shape optimization gains, and how applying one method changes the benefit of another. From this analysis, we derive VLA-Triage, guidelines for stacking optimizations under latency budgets. The guidelines identify which stages can provide the required savings and which combinations need testing in the deployed execution mode.

When to Ask for Help: Uncertainty-Triggered Aerial Refinement for Off-Road Navigation

When to Ask for Help: Uncertainty-Triggered Aerial Refinement for Off-Road Navigation

Pranay Samineni、Adam Thorpe、John Chubick、Anand Balakrishnan、Christian Ellis 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Heterogeneous air-ground teams can use aerial sensing to address the limited range and viewpoint of ground-vehicle sensors, but deciding when the UGV needs assistance and what information the UAV should collect remains a challenge. This paper presents a planner-conditioned air-ground assistance method that evaluates how uncertain map information affects the UGV's current MPPI rollouts. When navigation remains degraded, we score the same rollouts under resolved map hypotheses, select the map layer with the largest useful improvement, request the corresponding aerial measurement, fuse the returned information, and replan. Unlike approaches driven by fixed support roles, task structure, or uncertainty alone, our method conditions assistance on whether resolving uncertainty improves predicted ground navigation. Across 180 simulation trials on three off-road routes, the method achieves 98.9% goal attainment while reducing mean UAV flight time by 87.7% relative to continuous greedy aerial mapping. Aggregated across the routes, each second of estimated UAV flight time saves 2.94 s of UGV navigation time. Field experiments spanning approximately 2 km of UGV operation demonstrate uncertainty resolution and successful navigation recovery.

Which Physical Tactile Cues Matter for Contact-Rich Imitation Learning? A Controlled Real-Robot Study

Which Physical Tactile Cues Matter for Contact-Rich Imitation Learning? A Controlled Real-Robot Study

Xuhao Qin、Xiyan Huang、Haoyu Dong、Xi Zhang、Chenxi Xiao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-based tactile sensors capture multiple aspects of physical interaction within a single image stream, including contact appearance, geometry, indentation depth, and tangential deformation. However, existing visuo-tactile policies often directly process tactile observations as images, making it difficult to determine which physical cues contribute to policy learning. This paper presents a controlled real-robot study that compares physically interpretable tactile signals using the LeRobot platform, enhanced with our visuo-tactile perception system. From the same tactile stream, we derive tactile RGB images, normal/depth maps, and marker-flow fields, representing contact appearance, geometry, and tangential deformation, respectively. Using identical task demonstrations and the same policy backbone, we evaluate the individual and combined contributions of these tactile cues. We further compare tactile encoders and visuo-tactile fusion strategies. Evaluation across 720 real-robot trials yields four key findings: (1) tactile perception substantially improves the overall success rate from 57.8% to 78.9%, although its contribution varies across task stages; (2) marker flow is the most effective individual cue, achieving a success rate of 74.4% and playing a particularly important role in regulating established contact; (3) cue-factorized encoding consistently improves performance, highlighting that different physical cues require tailored processing techniques; and (4) although the effectiveness of tactile fusion is task-dependent, cross-attention offers a lightweight solution with performance comparable to that of pretrained tactile encoders.

Reflection-Robust Stereo Visual-Inertial Odometry via Kalman-Filtered Ground-Height Estimation

Reflection-Robust Stereo Visual-Inertial Odometry via Kalman-Filtered Ground-Height Estimation

Uihyun Hwang、Sungjae Shin、Dongjae Kim、Hyun Myung

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual-inertial odometry relies on visual features that correspond to 3D points with consistent projections across sequential frames. In reflective indoor-floor environments, however, specular floor patterns can be detected and triangulated as virtual features below the physical ground plane, producing unreliable feature associations and inconsistent visual residuals. This paper proposes a lightweight reflection-aware front-end for feature-based stereo VIO. Rather than suppressing all floor-region features, the proposed method estimates the ground height in real time and rejects only feature candidates whose inferred 3D positions are below the estimated ground height. A floor-region mask restricts the validation process to reflection-prone regions, while stereo height uncertainty and pose uncertainty are used to construct frame-level ground-height measurements. A scalar Kalman filter with Normalized Innovation Squared (NIS) gating then provides a stable ground-height estimate, and an uncertainty-aware safety margin is used for selective below-ground virtual feature rejection. Experiments on real reflective-floor indoor sequences show that the proposed front-end generally improves trajectory accuracy in reflection-dominant environments while maintaining accurate ground-height estimates and recovering from actual ground-height changes.

2026-11-04

FWIK: Multi-Waypoint IK Selection via Lookahead Graph Search

FWIK: Multi-Waypoint IK Selection via Lookahead Graph Search

Jinglan Cao、Guohui Zhong、Chengwei Zhang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

An ordered sequence of end-effector poses can give a redundant robot arm multiple valid joint configurations at each waypoint. Inverse kinematics (IK) computes these configurations. Choosing a configuration only for the next waypoint can leave the arm poorly placed for later waypoints. This may increase planned motion time or cause a later planning failure. This paper presents Future-Window Inverse Kinematics (FWIK), an online method that selects IK solutions by considering a small window of future waypoints. FWIK generates several IK candidates, connects them in a layered joint-space graph, and ranks candidate paths with a simple motion-time estimate. A learned model removes paths whose transitions are likely to cause self-collision. A standard MoveIt 2 planner then checks the next-waypoint IK goals from the remaining paths in ranked order, and the first goal that yields a valid trajectory is selected for execution. We evaluate FWIK in simulation on two 7-DoF manipulators, the Franka Emika Panda and the KUKA LBR iiwa. Each method-planner setting contains five repetitions of 100 sequences with 50 waypoints. Across Pilz, RRT-Connect, CHOMP, and STOMP, FWIK completes 96.8-100% of sequences and gives the shortest planned trajectory duration in all eight robot-planner settings. Its mean duration is 12.8-13.1% lower than STAMPEDE, 22.5-25.6% lower than IKLink, and 27.5-37.2% lower than retry-based TRAC-IK. The results show that a small future window can improve sequence completion and reduce planned motion time while leaving final trajectory validation to the motion planner.

目标检测/分割机器人视觉医学图像
2026-11-03

Beyond Accuracy: Information-Theoretic Decoder Evaluation Under Latent Intent

Beyond Accuracy: Information-Theoretic Decoder Evaluation Under Latent Intent

Tasha Kim、Benjamin Ballyk、Anson Lei、Ingmar Posner、Oiwi Parker Jones 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

A central challenge in assistive brain-to-robot control is decoding a user's latent intent from complex and noisy brain signals. Prior approaches evaluate decoders as single-trial classifiers with accuracy-based metrics, but this neglects the inherently sequential nature of assistive control. Since a planner must integrate intermittent, noisy decoder outputs over time, single-step accuracy can only be a proxy when predicting downstream task performance. We formalize brain-to-robot control as a partially observable Markov decision process (POMDP), and prove that there is a sharp phase transition: regret is uniformly bounded across the horizon as long as pairs of goals are all decoder-distinguishable, but grows linearly otherwise. We show that a single channel statistic, the worst-pair Chernoff information $Γ_{\min}$ sets this boundary. Then, we derive a closed-form regret predictor $U_{\mathrm{id}}(C)$, that can be computed from any confusion matrix in $O(K^2)$ time. Across two domains spanning orthogonal information channels, we show that $U_{\mathrm{id}}$ predicts cumulative planning regret beyond what accuracy can explain. We then highlight a pathological accuracy trap, in which two decoders have identical validation accuracy 35.1% on real EEG data (BNCI2014001 subject 5) but result in a difference of $9.6\times$ in held-out regret, which $U_{\mathrm{id}}$ predicts. Same-signal control confirms $U_{\mathrm{id}}$'s advantage with decoders that span independent feature channels. Ultimately, our work provides a drop-in diagnostic to evaluate and select sensors and decoders in sequential assistive control.

Contact-Implicit Whole-Body Trajectory Optimization for Dynamic Humanoid Locomotion on Parameterizable Uneven Terrain

Contact-Implicit Whole-Body Trajectory Optimization for Dynamic Humanoid Locomotion on Parameterizable Uneven Terrain

Roberto Mauceri、Stefano Dafarra、Giulio Romualdi、Marco Gabiccini、Daniele Pucci 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This letter presents a trajectory planner for humanoid robots to perform dynamic motions on complex, parameterizable terrains using a contact-implicit whole-body optimization framework. The planner generates feasible movements without predefined contact sequences, considering the robot's full dynamics and kinematics. To plan bipedal locomotion on steps, ramps, and other terrains, we propose a mathematical model to approximate ground geometry with a smooth surface. The rigid interaction between the robot and the ground is modeled via novel contact constraints, extending the domain of applicability from flat and horizontal surfaces to any arbitrary smooth surface. Effectiveness is demonstrated using nonlinear programming to plan motions on various terrain topologies. We also explore dynamic trajectory generation by embedding high-level requirements in the cost function and the initial guess. Preliminary feasibility tests were conducted over non-coplanar terrains, both in simulation and on the real robot ErgoCub.

2026-11-04

Detecting Silent Action-Interface Mismatch with Calibrated Relational World Models

Detecting Silent Action-Interface Mismatch with Calibrated Relational World Models

Rui Zhu、Xurui Li、Haixu Tang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

A robot action can satisfy shape and range constraints yet carry the wrong semantics: axes may be permuted, signs inverted, rotations rescaled, or gripper polarity reversed. We study learned state-action-effect relations as offline contracts for detecting these mismatches and ranking their likely fields. Clean held-out transitions calibrate alarms; a fixed inverse vocabulary supports closed-set localization. On ten LIBERO Spatial tasks comprising 432 source episodes, a capacity-controlled comparison at 2.5% empirical clean false-positive rate gives 46.1% recall for a ridge forward residual, 61.0% for a four-component joint Gaussian mixture, and 65.7% for the exact conditional of that same mixture. The joint score retains a 14.9 percentage-point advantage over ridge, while conditioning improves recall by a further 4.7 points. Joint scoring instead has higher localization: 95.8% versus 94.6%. These matched-rate comparisons are retrospective diagnostics, not deployment calibration guarantees. Earlier Object/Goal results broaden tasks within the LIBERO convention; DROID, all three HYDRA tasks, short temporal faults, small offsets, exact repair, and simulated mitigation fail their respective gates, while xArm contributes one task only. The evidence supports a bounded offline diagnostic and a detection-localization tradeoff, rather than joint-density superiority or cross-robot safety.

Reinforcement Learning Enables Scaling Generalist Robot Policy Improvement

Reinforcement Learning Enables Scaling Generalist Robot Policy Improvement

Michal Korniak、Marco Bagatella、Ralf Römer、Michal Nauman、Yarden As 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The introduction of generalist robot policies has enabled a single, large model to learn to perform a variety of tasks from a vast pre-training corpus. While these models often exhibit powerful zero-shot generalization capabilities, they typically require an adaptation phase to achieve sufficient performance in deployment. Iterated self-improvement via reinforcement learning (RL) has demonstrated promise in enabling this adaptation, yet such approaches have been largely explored in single-task settings - where the goal is to simply improve on a narrow deployment task - which lies in stark contrast to the multi-task capabilities of the base policy. In this work we ask whether generalist robot policies are capable of multi-task, generalist improvement in deployment - targeting many diverse tasks simultaneously - and what algorithmic recipe enables this. To answer this question, we first develop a set of structured evaluations to test generalist improvement capabilities, and systematically evaluate algorithmic and practical design choices regarding value estimation, policy extraction and resource allocation. We show that not only can generalist policies be improved online across diverse tasks, but that the right recipe can enable generalization and increase performance on tasks not considered during online training. We characterize when this generalization occurs and show that both the training task distribution and the use of value-based RL are critical. To the best of our knowledge, this is among the first evidence for online generalist improvement for robot foundation models.

Jev with Foresight: Safe Language-Guided Autonomous Driving via a Language World Model

Jev with Foresight: Safe Language-Guided Autonomous Driving via a Language World Model

Yansong Qu、Jiancong Chen、Samuel Labi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Large language models understand passengers' driving commands, but as driving agents they reason in generated text for seconds and must infer each action's consequences implicitly from the scene. We present Jev-AD, which pairs Jev, a typed decision model that answers predefined questions with probabilities, not generated text, with a language world model that predicts for every candidate action how nearby vehicles move over the next two seconds. Code turns these predictions into foreseen consequences, which Jev compares instead of inferring them from coordinates. In simulated highway merging (500 car-episodes), this foresight lowers the same decision model's collision rate from 26.0% to 2.4% at 0.25 seconds per decision, and Jev-AD achieves the passenger's intent in 93% of free-form command trials. In 500 replayed real-world NGSIM episodes, foresight lowers the collision rate from 48.2% to 2.8%, and the world model, learned from this traffic, foresees 93% of dangerous actions. Project page: https://ys-qu.github.io/jev-ad-website.

Robots in the City: Persona-Based Perceptions of Urban Robots

Robots in the City: Persona-Based Perceptions of Urban Robots

Keonhee Jang、Kanghoon Lee、Seokwoo Yun、Namwoo Kim、Yoonjin Yoon 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pedestrians increasingly encounter robots in urban public spaces without interacting with them, but how robots change the perceived safety of a street remains unclear. We examine how robot presence and type affect perceived safety across urban contexts. We generate 18 images of Seoul streets that vary in time of day, road class, and robot condition (no robot, a delivery robot, or a humanoid). Two multimodal large language models (MLLMs), Qwen3.8-27B and Gemma4-26B, each conditioned on 500 synthetic personas, choose the safer image in each pair, and 25 human respondents complete the same task. Both models prefer streets without robots and favor the delivery robot over the humanoid. Robots are chosen more often at night and less often by older personas, whereas road class has smaller effects. In the model rationales, the delivery robot appears as a physical obstacle, whereas the humanoid appears as either a threat or a source of protection. Like humans, both models chose robots more often at night, but chose the humanoid over the delivery robot far less often, and Gemma showed a night effect for the humanoid about three times that of humans. These results show that the effect of a robot on perceived safety depends on when it appears, and that persona-conditioned MLLMs capture only part of human judgments.

2026-11-04

Diversity-Aware Experience Replay via Statistical Leverage Scores

Diversity-Aware Experience Replay via Statistical Leverage Scores

Shaifalee Saxena、Alexander Scheinker

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Off-policy deep reinforcement learning trains on random samples from a replay buffer of past experience. In physical robot settings, that buffer is dominated by routine: thousands of near-identical transitions from normal operation, while the rare events that define the edges of the task are rarely sampled. We propose scoring every stored transition by its statistical leverage, which measures how unusual it is relative to the buffer's own data, computed from the stored numbers alone, with no labels, rewards, or value network. We draw each mini-batch from a mixture of this score and uniform sampling. First, the natural way to build the score along the directions where the data varies most turns out to be blind when actions are stored as one-hot indicators, because indicator columns always win the variance ranking; we instead select the feature columns with the heaviest tails (largest kurtosis) where small far-away populations are revealed. Second, the importance weights that prioritized replay uses to keep gradients unbiased cancel the method exactly: with full correction, training matches uniform sampling, so we drop the correction and deliberately optimize the rebalanced objective. Third, when the rare situations of a log are relational, visible only in how a stretch of behavior relates to its goal, not in any single stored coordinate-per-transition scores are blind; applying the same tail rule to short trajectory windows described relative to their goal restores the signal, and on a three-joint arm that must push an object around an obstacle it raises the rare-task success from 0.49 to 0.69 against a labeled-oracle ceiling of 0.74.

目标检测/分割生成模型/扩散机器人视觉

GRASP: Gradient-Guided Sampling for Contact-Rich Physics-Based Motion Retargeting

GRASP: Gradient-Guided Sampling for Contact-Rich Physics-Based Motion Retargeting

Yang Liu、Tong Wang、Yiming Bao、Heng Yang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We study physics-based retargeting of human demonstrations for robot learning: finding robot controls that reproduce reference motions under contact dynamics. We propose GRASP (Gradient Recovery from Archived Samples for Proposals), which combines sampling-based exploration with gradient-guided refinement without requiring a differentiable simulator. GRASP-TO extends tempered sequential Monte Carlo by fitting local quadratic cost models to archived rollouts and using their gradients to guide Markov chain Monte Carlo rejuvenation. Metropolis-Hastings correction preserves the target distribution for each fixed proposal kernel, and a population analysis connects the recovered gradients to Gaussian smoothing. GRASP-MPC scales this optimizer to long demonstrations through warm-started receding-horizon planning. Experiments on dexterous hands and humanoids show accurate retargeting with a shared tracking objective and no task-specific contact rewards, including 92.8% success on 1352 bimanual OakInk2 demonstrations. The refined references improve PPO tracking in pen spinning and humanoid-object interaction. We also demonstrate dexterous manipulation and whole-body humanoid motion on real hardware.

cuRoboV2-Fast: Fast Torque-Limited Collision-Free Motion Planning

cuRoboV2-Fast: Fast Torque-Limited Collision-Free Motion Planning

Balakumar Sundaralingam、Adithyavairavan Murali、Stan Birchfield

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We present cuRoboV2-Fast, a GPU-accelerated planner that reduces both planning latency and execution time for collision-free, payload-aware, torque-limited motions. B-spline trajectory optimization reduces the required iterations, while accelerated robot computations and differentiable inverse dynamics reduce refinement cost and accommodate changing payloads. TimeOpt optimizes traversal speed along the fixed path under sampled velocity, acceleration, jerk, and torque constraints, exploiting local spline support to solve banded Newton systems efficiently on the GPU. With a 3,kg payload, cuRoboV2-Fast achieves 4.4 ms mean planning time on RTX 5090, 14.7x faster than cuRoboV2. Our method on Jetson AGX Orin plans faster than cuRoboV1 + TOPPRA on RTX 5090. A stereo-camera-based deployment on YAM achieves 5.93 s mean completed pick-and-drop cycles, 34.6% shorter than cuRoboV2. Code (Apache 2.0): www.github.com/NVlabs/curobo , Project Page: www.research.nvidia.com/labs/lpr/curobov2-fast .

Rethinking Self-Repair in Code-as-Policy Agents: What Drives Retry Success?

Rethinking Self-Repair in Code-as-Policy Agents: What Drives Retry Success?

Siddharth Aphale、Ayushman Singh

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Code-as-policy agents often use execution feedback to retry failed programs. Additional sampling and task guidance can also improve success, leaving the contribution of failure-specific feedback unclear. We study this question in simulated robot manipulation. A fixed lookup selects feedback templates using failure labels and the API variant. A frozen code model generates a fresh program after each scene reset. On the same 252 initial failures, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B recovers 85.3% within five retries, compared with 68.3% for resampling with the same retry cap. The advantage persists under fixed total-token budgets. Feedback from other episodes and fixed task guidance yield similar recovery point estimates, with no detected benefit from matching the coarse failure label. In a separate study, a fixed guided prompt used from the first attempt achieves an observed overall success rate similar to that of template-based feedback. Template-based feedback also lowers recovery on a nut-insertion task requiring a rim grasp, consistent with an incompatible center-grasp instruction. Evaluations of robot self-repair should include resampling, mismatched feedback, and concise, task-compatible static guidance as baselines.

A Frame-Level View and Image-Quality Annotation Tool for Fetal Echocardiography: Comparison with Spreadsheet Annotation

A Frame-Level View and Image-Quality Annotation Tool for Fetal Echocardiography: Comparison with Spreadsheet Annotation

Mohamed Azzam、Ziyang Xu、Quinlan Couch、Zoe Keese、Andrea Cadlo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Objective: To develop the Cardiologist Annotation Tool (CAT) for frame-level cardiac view and image-quality annotation in fetal echocardiography and compare it with spreadsheet-based annotation. Methods: CAT provides cine playback, frame navigation, and keyboard-based labeling without manual frame-range entry. Experts annotated 25 studies using both methods, and spreadsheet annotations were audited for predefined data-entry errors. In a randomized crossover study, six medical students annotated two normal studies, one with each method, against an expert reference. Results: Among 1,746 spreadsheet-annotated files, 6.2% contained major or critical data-entry errors, more frequently in congenital heart disease than normal studies (10.9% vs 3.4%). CAT yielded more annotated view segments per clip (3.6 vs 1.5). Rates of labeled and correctly labeled frames per minute were 1.77 and 1.68 times those with spreadsheets, respectively (both P = 0.031), with gains in all six students. No detectable difference in view-label accuracy was observed. Conclusion: CAT eliminates manual frame-range entry and enables finer-grained, structured annotation of fetal echocardiography, providing a practical approach to preparing datasets for AI research.

Objective: To develop the Cardiologist Annotation Tool (CAT) for frame-level cardiac view and image-quality annotation in fetal echocardiography and compare it with spreadsheet-based annotation. Correct Robots Before They Make Mistakes: Proactive Human-in-the-Loop Intervention via Preference Learning

Correct Robots Before They Make Mistakes: Proactive Human-in-the-Loop Intervention via Preference Learning

Xinkai Jiang、Jing Jiang、Hongyi Zhou、Pankhuri Vanjani、Ahmad Baki 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper presents Proactive Human-in-the-Loop (PHIL), a framework for proactive policy correction and preference-based post-training. PHIL enables operators to preview policy-predicted future motion and intervene before anticipated undesirable actions. During takeover, PHIL continues querying the policy and displaying its predicted trajectories in the physical workspace through an extended reality (XR) interface. PHIL pairs rejected policy predictions with corrective action chunks subsequently executed from the same physical state over the same horizon, and preference-based post-training encourages the policy to favor these corrections. This paper evaluates PHIL on four real-world manipulation tasks using a diffusion policy and a pretrained vision-language-action policy. With five correction episodes per condition, PHIL increases mean success rates across the evaluated tasks and demonstration budgets from 44.0% to 70.3% for the diffusion policy and from 57.8% to 76.9% for X-VLA, compared with HG-DAgger. Separately, policies trained with proactive corrections show fewer unnecessary recovery behaviors in successful episodes. Project page: https://intuitive-robots.github.io/PHIL-page/.

2026-11-03

ELA: End-to-End Latent Action Policy Learning from Video Demonstrations

ELA: End-to-End Latent Action Policy Learning from Video Demonstrations

Cheng-Chun Hsu、Peter Stone

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot imitation learning is limited by the scarcity of action-labeled data, while action-free demonstrations such as videos are abundant. Recent latent-action methods learn action-relevant representations from observation transitions, but they do not optimize latent actions for downstream control, leading to latent representations that are difficult for policies to model and difficult to accurately decode into continuous robot actions. This paper introduces ELA, an end-to-end framework for learning from video demonstrations via latent actions jointly optimized for policy learning and action decoding. ELA jointly trains a latent action model, latent action policy, and action decoder, enabling policies to leverage abundant video data with only a handful of action-labeled robot demonstrations. Experiments on simulated and real-world manipulation tasks show that ELA consistently outperforms prior latent action baselines. Moreover, it integrates seamlessly with existing imitation learning methods through lightweight 4M-parameter action decoders, requiring no changes to policy architectures or training objectives. Additional materials can be found on: https://end2end-latent-action.github.io/

A Layered View of LLM Support in Underwater Robotics

A Layered View of LLM Support in Underwater Robotics

Federico Celi、Leonardo Zacchini、Gabriele Ferri

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This extended abstract discusses a specific and, in our experience, practically relevant role for large language models (LLMs) in underwater robotics. Our main premise is that, in underwater fielding missions, the immediate difficulty is often not the synthesis of new low-level control laws, but the operation of complex robotic systems under limited communications, partial observability, and expensive field time. In particular, a significant fraction of the effort during sea trials is devoted to fault diagnosis, telemetry interpretation, mission adjustment, and coordination across heterogeneous assets and operators. We argue that LLMs may be useful at this higher operational level, especially when grounded in structured vehicle state, alarms, logs, mission context, and existing planning or validation tools. At the same time, current limitations in grounding, consistency, and causal reasoning make these systems lack the predictability required to guarantee direct control, especially in real-world critical applications, although we do not exclude a future evolution in this direction. This viewpoint suggests a layered architecture in which conventional autonomy remains responsible for control, perception, estimation, and validated mission execution, whereas LLM-based modules serve as an additional support layer for debugging, operator interaction, constrained replanning support, orchestration, and post-mission analysis.

Our main premise is that, in underwater fielding missions, the immediate difficulty is often not the synthesis of new low-level control laws, but the operation of complex robotic systems under limited communications, partial observability, and expensive field time. In particular, a significant fraction of the effort during sea trials is devoted to fault diagnosis, telemetry interpretation, mission adjustment, and coordination across heterogeneous assets and operators. We argue that LLMs may be useful at this higher operational level, especially when grounded in structured vehicle state, alarms, logs, mission context, and existing planning or validation tools. At the same time, current limitations in grounding, consistency, and causal reasoning make these systems lack the predictability required to guarantee direct control, especially in real-world critical applications, although we do not exclude a future evolution in this direction. This viewpoint suggests a layered architecture in which conventional autonomy remains responsible for control, perception, estimation, and validated mission execution, whereas LLM-based modules serve as an additional support layer for debugging, operator interaction, constrained replanning support, orchestration, and post-mission analysis.

Towards Insight-Centric Data Spaces: Integrating Visual Analytics and Decentralized Data Ecosystems

Towards Insight-Centric Data Spaces: Integrating Visual Analytics and Decentralized Data Ecosystems

Christina Faßbender、Benjamin Greiner、Benedikt T. Arnold、Georgios Toubekis、Christoph Lange 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual analytics and dataspaces address complementary challenges in modern data ecosystems: visual analytics enables interactive exploration and human-centered reasoning over complex data, dataspaces provide decentralized infrastructures for sovereign and collaborative data sharing. Both fields have largely evolved independently, resulting in a gap between data management and data understanding in distributed environments.We explore their intersection and argue that integration is essential for next-generation data ecosystems. Visual analytics acts as a cognitive layer for dataspaces by making data availability, quality, provenance, and exchange processes observable and interpretable, while dataspaces reshape visual analytics by introducing decentralized, heterogeneous, and governed data environments. We illustrate this interplay through a semi-synthetic cultural dataspace scenario analyzing exchange patterns and metadata quality across a federated ecosystem. Based on these insights, we identify key open challenges and outline a vision of insight-centric data spaces, in which data management and analysis are tightly integrated into a unified, human-centered environment.

Fluidic Body: Swarm Robots as a Fluid Extension of the Hand

Fluidic Body: Swarm Robots as a Fluid Extension of the Hand

Michikuni Eguchi、Shigeo Yoshida、Mai Nishimura、Takefumi Hiraki

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Swarm robots have attracted growing interest as a physical interface for human--computer interaction, since a swarm can reshape its formation to fit the objects and surroundings it works with. For a human operator, however, adapting the swarm's behavior remains difficult. Most existing methods require the user to specify where the swarm should go, and they offer no intuitive way to change the collective's behavior as each task demands. We present Fluidic Body, a system that lets a person operate a swarm of fluid-imitating robots as an extension of the hand. The system places a virtual fluid between the hand and the swarm, and the user adapts the swarm's behavior to each task by changing the fluid's properties through simple hand movements. When the user opens the hand, the swarm becomes soft enough to deform and flow around obstacles. Closing the hand turns it into a hard, cohesive body that can push objects. We evaluated the controller's performance and its operability in two simulation experiments and one demonstration with physical robots. First, our controller handled both obstacle avoidance and object transportation better than an existing method that behaves the same way in every task. Second, in a VR user study, 17 participants completed the combined avoidance-and-transportation task faster and more reliably than with that method. They also reported a lower workload and a stronger sense of agency. Finally, this experience carried over to physical robots in a real-world demonstration (88 participants). These results suggest that treating a swarm as a fluid, a material people already understand, allows a robot swarm to work as a deformable extension of the human body.

Compounding Physical RSI: Turning Execution Failure into Inherited Capability

Compounding Physical RSI: Turning Execution Failure into Inherited Capability

Yixuan Yang、Qiang Chen、Run Shao、Shuwei Dong、Wenhao Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generalist robot policies are trained on static offline datasets, yet physical manipulation unfolds sequentially across stages. Because a weakness in a downstream stage remains unobservable until the policy passes earlier ones, each resolved bottleneck exposes the next hidden failure mode. This staged structure calls for Recursive Self-Improvement (RSI), in which a policy iteratively executes, diagnoses failures, and updates itself. Moreover, because task completion behaves like a product of stage-level pass rates, recursive gains can multiply across generations. In physical environments, three bottlenecks obstruct compounding: terminal execution feedback rarely localizes the stage that caused a failure, failed trajectories do not supply the corrective action, and updating shared parameters can degrade previously mastered skills. We propose Compounding Physical RSI, a self-evolving policy framework that turns execution failures into heritable capability. First, automated capability-gap assessment traces failed rollouts to the task stage where progression breaks down and formulates a structured improvement objective. Second, conditioned on this objective, our remedy stage curates validated, stage-relevant experience and refines the policy via behavior-regularized offline reinforcement learning, without requiring costly online rollouts at each optimization step. Third, to safeguard existing skills, our retention stage constructs weight-space candidates between the inherited reference policy and the refined policy, evaluates them across the task suite, and promotes the best candidate as the reference state for the next generation. Evaluated across all 42 tasks of the RoboDojo-Sim benchmark, CSU-AI-07 ranks first on the leaderboard with a three-seed mean Overall Score of 26.15 and Success Rate of 20.70%, outperforming the next-best entry by 5.92 points and 5.82 percentage points.

SCRUB: Contact-Based Surface-Frame Estimation for Force-Motion Control with an Armless Quadruped

SCRUB: Contact-Based Surface-Frame Estimation for Force-Motion Control with an Armless Quadruped

Sourav Selvaraj、Juan Pablo Espinosa Chessal、Mahdi Agheli

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Wiping, sanding, and inspection robots press a sliding tool on a surface with controlled force. Controlling that force needs the surface normal, which is unknown on real-world, unmapped surfaces. Surface-normal estimators can help estimate the surface normal, but they either need a friction model or assume that the instantaneous tool velocity lies along the surface; however, walking violates this assumption. We propose SCRUB, a contact-based surface-frame estimator, which lets an armless quadruped slide a body-mounted tool along near-vertical surfaces in the absence of known, axis-aligned, or vision-predicted normals. SCRUB's predictive controller presses and slides the tool using a Kalman-filter estimate of the normal from two cues: 1) A force cue measures the estimate's tilt across the motion. If friction directly opposes the motion, the cue is zero exactly when that tilt is zero, for any friction coefficient. However, if the cue's sliding direction is wrong, friction appears as a false tilt. 2) A velocity cue measures tilt along the motion. Walking and pressing move the tool in and out. SCRUB therefore subtracts the commanded press rate from the tool velocity, averages the result over each gait cycle, and treats this mean as lying along the surface. On Unitree Go1 hardware at nominal speed and load, SCRUB completed every wall traverse and column orbit. No orbit completed with either cue removed, without gait averaging, or with a fixed normal.

Playing Soccer Like Humans Do: Learning Versatile and Agile Humanoid Soccer via Latent Motion Priors

Playing Soccer Like Humans Do: Learning Versatile and Agile Humanoid Soccer via Latent Motion Priors

Haoyu Shao、Yunshen Wang、Jingze Zhang、Shaohang Zhu、Yong-Lu Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Humanoid soccer requires a robot to seamlessly combine agile locomotion, ball manipulation, and impact-rich contact dynamics across a continuous, dynamic behavioral spectrum. Yet existing approaches either cover only a limited set of skills, or forego motion priors entirely and produce unnatural behaviors, leaving the combination of a unified skill set and human-like motion quality unaddressed. We present soccerbot, a hierarchical reinforcement learning framework that resolves both limitations through a structured latent motion prior distilled from large-scale human soccer motion capture data. By constraining policy learning within this dynamically plausible motion manifold, a single skill-conditioned policy naturally learns to perform moving, dribbling, trapping, and kicking in a unified formulation, while inherently producing human-like motion and fluid transitions during commanded skill switching. Augmented by robustness-oriented simulation design, soccerbot transfers zero-shot to the Unitree G1 humanoid robot, demonstrating agile, natural, and robust execution of the full soccer skill repertoire under real-world conditions.
